1	# Architecture
2	
3	How AgentCenter fits together today. For *what we plan to build next*, see [`plan.md`](./plan.md). For deployment, see [`deploy.md`](./deploy.md).
4	
5	## Topology
6	
7	```text
8	Browser ──── Vercel (Next.js 16) ──── Neon (Postgres)
9	                ├── App Router pages (RSC + client islands)
10	                ├── /api/auth/...        Better Auth cookie sessions
11	                ├── /api/v1/...          Public registry API (CLI)
12	                ├── /api/upload/sign     R2 presigned PUT
13	                └── /api/inngest         Inngest webhook
14	                                              │
15	Browser ─── direct PUT ──→  Cloudflare R2 ←──┘
16	                                              │
17	                                          Inngest ──→ Neon + R2 (background jobs)
18	
19	CLI (Bun-built binary) ──── /api/v1/... (Bearer token via device-code flow)
20	```
21	
22	Web requests hit Vercel; the CLI hits the same Vercel deployment but talks only to the `/api/v1` surface. Bundles flow directly between the browser/CLI and R2 via signed URLs — Vercel never proxies binary content.
23	
24	## Web app layout
25	
26	The App Router tree is locale-segmented. URLs are always prefixed (`/en/...`, `/zh/...`); there is no implicit default.
27	
28	```text
29	app/
30	├── layout.tsx                 # html/body, font loaders, theme cookie
31	├── [locale]/
32	│   ├── layout.tsx             # next-intl provider, AppShell (sidebar + topbar)
33	│   ├── page.tsx               # home (featured + trending)
34	│   ├── extensions/
35	│   │   ├── page.tsx           # browse (filter bar + grid)
36	│   │   └── [slug]/page.tsx    # detail (README + metadata panel)
37	│   ├── publish/
38	│   │   ├── page.tsx           # dashboard (my extensions)
39	│   │   └── new/page.tsx       # upload wizard
40	│   ├── (auth)/sign-in,sign-up
41	│   └── cli/auth/page.tsx      # device-code authorization UI
42	└── api/                       # see "API surfaces" below
43	```
44	
45	### RSC vs client islands
46	
47	Server components render the static shell, fetch data via Drizzle, and stream HTML. Client components are reserved for interactive bits — filter chips, the install button, the upload wizard, the user menu — and are marked with `"use client"`. The pattern is "server frame, client islands."
48	
49	The filter bar (`components/filters/filter-bar.tsx`) is a server component but composes client islands (`FilterChips`, `SortSelect`, `DeptPicker`, `TagDrawer`). Each island reads/writes URL search params via `useFilterUrl`, so filter state lives in the URL — no global store, no context, and back/forward works.
50	
51	### i18n
52	
53	Locales live under `lib/i18n/messages/{en,zh}.json`. `next-intl` is configured with `localePrefix: "always"`. Static UI strings come from `useTranslations(...)`; bilingual content (extension `name` / `nameZh`, tag labels) is column-per-language in Postgres and resolved at the component level via `tagLabel(...)` / direct field access.
54	
55	### Theme
56	
57	`Editorial Ivory` (default) and `Dark`. The active theme is stored in a cookie (`theme`), read in the root layout, and applied as a `data-theme` attribute. The toggle (`ThemeSwitch`) writes the cookie and triggers `router.refresh()`.
58	
59	## Request flows
60	
61	### Browse
62	
63	```text
64	GET /en/extensions?category=skills&dept=eng.cloud&sort=stars
65	  └─ app/[locale]/extensions/page.tsx (RSC)
66	      ├─ parseFilters(searchParams)
67	      ├─ Promise.all([listExtensions, countFilteredExtensions, getTagsWithCounts])
68	      └─ <FilterBar tags={tags} /> + <ExtGrid items={items} />
69	```
70	
71	`buildExtensionWhere` / `buildExtensionOrder` in `lib/search/query.ts` compose Drizzle SQL clauses from filters; they're pure (no DB call), unit-tested, and shared with `/api/v1/extensions` so web and CLI see the same listing semantics.
72	
73	### Detail
74	
75	```text
76	GET /en/extensions/my-skill
77	  └─ app/[locale]/extensions/[slug]/page.tsx (RSC)
78	      ├─ getExtensionBySlug(slug)
79	      ├─ react-markdown + rehype-sanitize on the stored README
80	      └─ <ExtHero /> + <Markdown /> + <ExtMetadataPanel /> + <InstallButton />
81	```
82	
83	The README is raw markdown stored in Postgres and server-rendered with `rehype-sanitize`. The metadata side panel (homepage, repo, license, compatibility, taxonomy) is a server component fed from the same row.
84	
85	### Publish
86	
87	```text
88	Browser                          Vercel                          R2          Inngest
89	   │ form submit                    │                              │             │
90	   ├──── createDraftExtension ────► server action  ──► extensions row (status=draft)
91	   │                                │                              │             │
92	   ├──── upload bundle ─────────────│─── presigned PUT URL ───────►│             │
93	   │                                │                              │             │
94	   ├──── attachFile(versionId) ────► server action  ──► files row + extensionVersion
95	   │                                │                              │             │
96	   ├──── submitForReview ──────────► server action  ──► sendEvent("extension/scan.requested")
97	   │                                │                              │             │
98	   │                                │                              │  scan-bundle:
99	   │                                │                              │   download from R2,
100	   │                                │                              │   sha-256 checksum,
101	   │                                │                              │   parse manifest.toml,
102	   │                                │                              │   validate schema,
103	   │                                │                              │   mark version ready/rejected,
104	   │                                │                              │   sendEvent("extension/index.requested")
105	   │                                │                              │             │
106	   │                                │                              │  reindex-search:
107	   │                                │                              │   build search_vector,
108	   │                                │                              │   set extension visibility=published,
109	   │                                │                              │   revalidateTag("extensions")
110	```
111	
112	The upload goes browser → R2 directly (presigned PUT) — Vercel only signs the URL. The Inngest webhook lives at `/api/inngest`. Job source: `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` and `lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts`.
113	
114	A note on `scan-bundle`: download + checksum + manifest parsing live in a single `step.run` rather than separate steps, because Inngest serializes step return values across boundaries and `Buffer` doesn't survive that round-trip.
115	
116	### Install (CLI path)
117	
118	```text
119	agentcenter install my-skill
120	  ├─ GET /api/v1/extensions/my-skill                     metadata (name, version, category)
121	  ├─ GET /api/v1/extensions/my-skill/bundle              302 → signed R2 URL
122	  ├─ fetch(signed-url)                                   ZIP bytes
123	  ├─ unzip + write to ~/.claude/skills/my-skill/         (or configured agent path)
124	  └─ POST /api/v1/installs (Bearer token)                records install event, increments downloadsCount
125	```
126	
127	The web "Install" button takes the same conceptual path but goes through the `installExtension` server action instead of the public API: it bumps the same counters and records the same install event, so leaderboards stay consistent across surfaces.
128	
129	## Authentication
130	
131	Two parallel flows on top of one Better Auth instance:
132	
133	- **Web** — cookie sessions. `signIn.email()` from `authClient` (`better-auth/react`) sets the cookie; subsequent server components read the session via `getSession()`. Client components subscribe via `authClient.useSession()` for reactive updates.
134	- **CLI** — device-code flow on top of Better Auth's `verifications` table.
135	  1. CLI: `POST /api/v1/auth/device/code` → server creates two `verifications` rows (`dc:poll:<deviceCode>` and `dc:user:<userCode>`) and returns the user code.
136	  2. User: opens `/{locale}/cli/auth` in a browser, signs in, enters the user code; the page resolves user code → device code, marks the poll row authorized, and stores a session token in it.
137	  3. CLI: polls `POST /api/v1/auth/device/poll` every 5 s. When the row is authorized, the token is returned once and the row is deleted.
138	  4. CLI persists the token in `~/.config/agentcenter/credentials.json` (mode 0600) and uses it as a `Bearer` for authenticated v1 calls.
139	
140	The token is a Better Auth session token — the same kind a browser cookie carries — so the same `sessions` table backs both flows.
141	
142	## API surfaces
143	
144	| Path | Purpose |
145	|---|---|
146	| `/api/auth/[...all]` | Better Auth — session cookies, sign-in, sign-up, sign-out (web) |
147	| `/api/v1/...` | Public registry API consumed by the CLI ([`api.md`](./api.md)) |
148	| `/api/upload/sign` | Web-internal — issues an R2 presigned PUT URL for the upload wizard |
149	| `/api/inngest` | Webhook — Inngest dispatches `scan-bundle` and `reindex-search` here |
150	
151	Only `/api/v1/*` is treated as a contract; the others are implementation details.
152	
153	## Data flow at a glance
154	
155	```text
156	publish wizard ──→ extensions (draft) ──→ files + extensionVersions ──→ Inngest scan
157	                                                                           │
158	                          extensions (published, search_vector) ←──── reindex-search
159	                                            │
160	                          listExtensions (browse) ── installExtension / install API
161	                                                            │
162	                                                       installs row
163	                                                       collections "installed"
164	                                                       downloadsCount++
165	```
166	
167	Every write that affects what users see in the listing (publish, install) ends with a `revalidateTag` so the next browse render hits fresh data.
168	
```

```
1	# Language
2	
3	Shared vocabulary for every suggestion this skill makes. Use these terms exactly — don't substitute "component," "service," "API," or "boundary." Consistent language is the whole point.
4	
5	## Terms
6	
7	**Module**
8	Anything with an interface and an implementation. Deliberately scale-agnostic — applies equally to a function, class, package, or tier-spanning slice.
9	_Avoid_: unit, component, service.
10	
11	**Interface**
12	Everything a caller must know to use the module correctly. Includes the type signature, but also invariants, ordering constraints, error modes, required configuration, and performance characteristics.
13	_Avoid_: API, signature (too narrow — those refer only to the type-level surface).
14	
15	**Implementation**
16	What's inside a module — its body of code. Distinct from **Adapter**: a thing can be a small adapter with a large implementation (a Postgres repo) or a large adapter with a small implementation (an in-memory fake). Reach for "adapter" when the seam is the topic; "implementation" otherwise.
17	
18	**Depth**
19	Leverage at the interface — the amount of behaviour a caller (or test) can exercise per unit of interface they have to learn. A module is **deep** when a large amount of behaviour sits behind a small interface. A module is **shallow** when the interface is nearly as complex as the implementation.
20	
21	**Seam** _(from Michael Feathers)_
22	A place where you can alter behaviour without editing in that place. The *location* at which a module's interface lives. Choosing where to put the seam is its own design decision, distinct from what goes behind it.
23	_Avoid_: boundary (overloaded with DDD's bounded context).
24	
25	**Adapter**
26	A concrete thing that satisfies an interface at a seam. Describes *role* (what slot it fills), not substance (what's inside).
27	
28	**Leverage**
29	What callers get from depth. More capability per unit of interface they have to learn. One implementation pays back across N call sites and M tests.
30	
31	**Locality**
32	What maintainers get from depth. Change, bugs, knowledge, and verification concentrate at one place rather than spreading across callers. Fix once, fixed everywhere.
33	
34	## Principles
35	
36	- **Depth is a property of the interface, not the implementation.** A deep module can be internally composed of small, mockable, swappable parts — they just aren't part of the interface. A module can have **internal seams** (private to its implementation, used by its own tests) as well as the **external seam** at its interface.
37	- **The deletion test.** Imagine deleting the module. If complexity vanishes, the module wasn't hiding anything (it was a pass-through). If complexity reappears across N callers, the module was earning its keep.
38	- **The interface is the test surface.** Callers and tests cross the same seam. If you want to test *past* the interface, the module is probably the wrong shape.
39	- **One adapter means a hypothetical seam. Two adapters means a real one.** Don't introduce a seam unless something actually varies across it.
40	
41	## Relationships
42	
43	- A **Module** has exactly one **Interface** (the surface it presents to callers and tests).
44	- **Depth** is a property of a **Module**, measured against its **Interface**.
45	- A **Seam** is where a **Module**'s **Interface** lives.
46	- An **Adapter** sits at a **Seam** and satisfies the **Interface**.
47	- **Depth** produces **Leverage** for callers and **Locality** for maintainers.
48	
49	## Rejected framings
50	
51	- **Depth as ratio of implementation-lines to interface-lines** (Ousterhout): rewards padding the implementation. We use depth-as-leverage instead.
52	- **"Interface" as the TypeScript `interface` keyword or a class's public methods**: too narrow — interface here includes every fact a caller must know.
53	- **"Boundary"**: overloaded with DDD's bounded context. Say **seam** or **interface**.
54	
```

Perfect. I have enough data. Let me compile the comprehensive report:

## Deepening Opportunities Report

### 1. `lib/data/` vs `lib/db/queries/` vs `lib/actions/` — Shallow Data & Query Layers

**What's there:** Three files in `/lib/data/` (`extensions.ts`, `departments.ts`, `collections.ts`) are mock seed data. `/lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` has pure query functions (`listExtensions`, `countFilteredExtensions`, `getExtensionBySlug`, etc.). `/lib/actions/install.ts` duplicates collection-lookup and counter-increment logic from the API route.

The call chain is: RSC (`extensions/page.tsx`) → `parseFilters()` + `listExtensions()` directly. Controllers (`app/api/v1/extensions`) also call `listExtensions()` directly. No data access module sits between; the query layer is the seam.

**Deletion test:** Delete `/lib/data/extensions.ts` — nothing breaks because it's just mock seed data. Delete `/lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` — callers are forced to either inline Drizzle or create a replacement module. The layer exists but adds no leverage; it's a thin wrapper over Drizzle with no policy. **Verdict: SHALLOW.**

**Deepening sketch:** The layer isn't earning its keep because it doesn't hide a *rule*. There's no "extension visibility" concept, no soft-delete semantics, no versioning logic. The query functions are pure selects. Make it deep by moving **filter interpretation & visibility rules** into a module with one interface: `getPublishedExtensions(filters, userDeptId): Promise<ExtensionItem[]>`. This module owns the invariant that `visibility = 'published'` is always applied, and that department fallback is always respected. Callers stop knowing about `buildExtensionWhere()` or visibility columns; they just ask for "published items matching these filters."

---

### 2. Install Flow Duplication — Shallow & Duplicated Module

**What's there:** `/lib/actions/install.ts` (web) and `/app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` (CLI) both:
- Query extension by ID (web) or slug (CLI)
- Get or create "installed" system collection
- Check if already installed
- Insert into `installs` table
- Increment `downloadsCount`

Lines 40–54 (install action) vs 51–64 (API route): identical sequence, only IDs differ.

**Deletion test:** Delete either one — the other must reimplement. Both sites leak knowledge of collections, counters, and the "already installed" check. **Verdict: SHALLOW & DUPLICATED.**

**Deepening sketch:** Extract a single **install recording module** at `/lib/actions/record-install.ts`:

```ts
export async function recordInstall(
  userId: string,
  extensionId: string,
  metadata: { source: "web" | "cli"; version: string }
): Promise<{ alreadyInstalled: boolean }>
```

This module owns:
- Fetching the extension (internal; callers don't choose ID vs slug)
- Collection semantics (internal)
- The counter increment (internal)
- Return shape

Both web action and API route call `recordInstall()` and let it handle the details. Callers stop knowing about `collections`, `isInCollection`, or `downloadsCount`; they delegate the *policy* of "what recording an install means" to one place.

---

### 3. Filter Pipeline — Filter State Scattered, Not Deep

**What's there:** 
- `/lib/validators/filters.ts`: defines `filtersSchema`, `parseFilters()`, types
- `/lib/search/query.ts`: `buildExtensionWhere()`, `buildExtensionOrder()` — pure SQL builders
- `/lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts`: client-side URL ↔ state syncing
- `components/filters/*`: UI islands (DeptPicker, FilterChips, etc.) each calling `useFilterUrl`

The data flow is: URL → `parseFilters()` (normalizes tags array) → `buildExtensionWhere()` (pure) → Drizzle. Each island reads/writes URL params directly; no filter state object lives in one place.

**Deletion test:** Delete `/lib/validators/filters.ts` — callers must reimplement `filtersSchema` and `parseFilters()`. Delete `/lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts` — each island reinvents URL sync. Neither owns the filter *semantics*. **Verdict: SHALLOW.**

**Deepening sketch:** The filter concept is real (validated, used for SQL, persisted in URL), but the module that *owns* it is fragmented. Create `/lib/filters/index.ts`:

```ts
export const FilterSchema = z.object({ /* ... */ });
export type Filters = z.infer<typeof FilterSchema>;

export function parseFiltersFromUrl(raw: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>): Filters
export function buildSqlWhere(filters: Filters, fallbackDeptId?: string): SQL | undefined
export function buildSqlOrder(sort: Filters["sort"]): SQL[]

// On the client side:
export function useFilterState() { /* replaces useFilterUrl but returns Filters, not raw URL ops */ }
```

One module defines what a filter *is*, how to parse it, validate it, turn it into SQL, and update the URL. Components stop reaching into URL params; they work through a "filter state" concept. `parseFilters` and `buildExtensionWhere` are internal implementation details.

---

### 4. Manifest Pipeline — Manifest Schema Duplicated

**What's there:**
- `/lib/validators/manifest.ts`: `ManifestFormSchema` (21 lines) for upload form validation
- `/lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts`: `BundleManifestSchema` (20 lines) for parsing manifest.toml from the downloaded ZIP

Both define `slug`, `name`, `version`, `category`, `scope`, + sub-shape for categorization. The form schema has stricter rules (e.g., slug regex), the bundle schema is looser. No shared definition.

**Deletion test:** Delete `/lib/validators/manifest.ts` — the form and API route lose their validation (must duplicate). Delete `/lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` — scanning loses its schema. Both are needed, both duplicate. **Verdict: SHALLOW & DUPLICATED.**

**Deepening sketch:** Create `/lib/validators/extension-manifest.ts` as the single source of truth:

```ts
export const ExtensionManifestSchema = z.object({
  extension: z.object({ /* shared fields */ }),
  categorization: z.object({ /* shared fields */ }),
});

// Export stricter variants for context-specific use:
export const FormManifestSchema = ExtensionManifestSchema.pick({/* fields for form */});
export const BundleManifestSchema = ExtensionManifestSchema.pick({/* fields for bundle */});
```

Or, simpler: one schema in `/lib/validators/extension-manifest.ts`, and both form + job import it. No duplication, one home for "what is a valid extension manifest."

---

### 5. CLI `installers/` — Real Seam, but Not Yet Polymorphic

**What's there:** `/cli/src/installers/index.ts` wraps `/installers/skills.ts` and dispatches on `category`. Both functions return promises; the category switch only leads to `installSkill()` in all cases (fallback comment on line 14: "treat everything else as a skill-style directory drop").

**Deletion test:** Delete the dispatcher — callers would directly call `installSkill()`. The adapter pattern is *hypothetical*; only one implementation exists. **Verdict: SHALLOW (adapter anticipating future categories that don't yet vary).**

**Deepening verdict:** This is fine as-is *for now*. Once `mcp`, `plugins`, `slash` have distinct install logic (symlinks, config merging, etc.), the dispatcher becomes real. Until then, it's premature. Leave it; it's a placeholder, not a leaky seam. No action needed.

---

### 6. R2 Storage — Already Deep

**What's there:** `/lib/storage/r2.ts` exports three functions: `generatePresignedPutUrl()`, `generatePresignedGetUrl()`, `bundleKey()`. All three are *pure API adapters* — they construct S3Client, issue presigned URL commands, and compose keys. The module hides credentials, region, bucket name, and signing logic. Callers never see AWS SDK details.

**Deletion test:** Delete it — all callers must adopt `S3Client` directly and manage credentials, endpoints, signing, and expiry themselves. Removing it *concentrates* complexity into the module. **Verdict: DEEP.**

No action needed.

---

### 7. Background Jobs — Event Interface is Shallow

**What's there:** `/lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` and `/lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts` are Inngest functions. They consume events (`extension/scan.requested`, `extension/index.requested`) with typed data (`versionId`, `fileId`). Each job's `step.run()` calls do inline DB updates (mark files scanned, set visibility, increment counters).

The event interface is thin (just IDs), but the job *body* is a sprawl of DB writes: `files.scanStatus`, `extensionVersions.status`, `extensionVersions.publishedAt`, `extensions.visibility`, `extensions.search_vector`, `extensions.published_at`. Callers (e.g., `publish.ts` triggering the event) don't know what fields will be updated; the job is opaque.

**Deletion test:** Delete `scan-bundle.ts` — publish flow breaks because no one scans bundles. Delete the `step.run()` inside — you must find another way to mark files and versions. The logic is tightly coupled to the job; no separate "mark version as ready" module exists. **Verdict: SHALLOW.**

**Deepening sketch:** Extract the DB state transitions into a separate module `/lib/extension-state/index.ts`:

```ts
export async function markVersionScanned(versionId: string, fileId: string, scan: ScanResult) {
  // owns: files.scanStatus, extensionVersions.status, checksumSha256, scanReport
}

export async function publishVersion(versionId: string) {
  // owns: extensionVersions.publishedAt, extensions.visibility, search_vector
}
```

Jobs call these functions instead of issuing raw SQL/Drizzle. Callers (and future jobs) delegate version lifecycle to one place. The event payload is now just IDs; the job orchestrates state transitions, not implements them.

---

## Summary — 3-5 Candidates for Deepening

1. **Extract filter semantics** (`lib/filters/`): Consolidate `parseFilters`, `buildExtensionWhere`, `buildExtensionOrder`, `useFilterUrl` under one interface. Callers work with `Filters` type, not raw URL params or SQL builders.

2. **Merge install logic** (`lib/actions/record-install.ts`): Single module owns the policy of recording an install (collection semantics, counter, duplication check). Web and CLI routes delegate to it.

3. **Unify manifest schemas** (`lib/validators/extension-manifest.ts`): One definition of a valid extension manifest. Form and scanner import and apply it. No duplication.

4. **Extract version state transitions** (`lib/extension-state/`): `markVersionScanned`, `publishVersion`, etc. Jobs delegate to these functions instead of writing raw SQL. Callers stop knowing about visibility columns, counter updates.

5. **Lift extension visibility into the query layer** (expand `lib/db/queries/extensions.ts`): Move `buildExtensionWhere` as an internal detail. Expose `getPublishedExtensions(filters, userDeptId)` — one interface, hides the rule that visibility is always checked.

The installer seam, R2 storage, and data mocks are fine as-is.
```

1	"use server";
2	
3	import { eq, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
4	
5	import { getSession } from "@/lib/auth/session";
6	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
7	import { installs } from "@/lib/db/schema/activity";
8	import { extensions } from "@/lib/db/schema/extension";
9	import {
10	  getOrCreateSystemCollection,
11	  isInCollection,
12	  upsertCollectionItem,
13	} from "@/lib/db/queries/collections";
14	
15	export type InstallResult =
16	  | { ok: true; alreadyInstalled: boolean }
17	  | { ok: false; error: "unauthenticated" | "not_found" | "server_error" };
18	
19	export async function installExtension(
20	  extensionId: string,
21	): Promise<InstallResult> {
22	  const session = await getSession();
23	  if (!session) return { ok: false, error: "unauthenticated" };
24	
25	  const [ext] = await db
26	    .select({ id: extensions.id })
27	    .from(extensions)
28	    .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId))
29	    .limit(1);
30	  if (!ext) return { ok: false, error: "not_found" };
31	
32	  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(
33	    session.user.id,
34	    "installed",
35	  );
36	
37	  const alreadyInstalled = await isInCollection(installedColId, extensionId);
38	
39	  if (!alreadyInstalled) {
40	    await db.insert(installs).values({
41	      id: crypto.randomUUID(),
42	      userId: session.user.id,
43	      extensionId,
44	      version: "latest",
45	      source: "web",
46	    });
47	
48	    await upsertCollectionItem(installedColId, extensionId);
49	
50	    // Increment counter atomically.
51	    await db
52	      .update(extensions)
53	      .set({ downloadsCount: sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} + 1` })
54	      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));
55	  }
56	
57	  return { ok: true, alreadyInstalled };
58	}
59

1	import { eq } from "drizzle-orm";
2	import { NextRequest } from "next/server";
3	import { z } from "zod/v4";
4	
5	import { authenticateBearerToken, jsonError } from "@/lib/api/auth";
6	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
7	import { installs } from "@/lib/db/schema/activity";
8	import { extensions } from "@/lib/db/schema/extension";
9	import {
10	  getOrCreateSystemCollection,
11	  isInCollection,
12	  upsertCollectionItem,
13	} from "@/lib/db/queries/collections";
14	
15	export const runtime = "nodejs";
16	
17	const InstallBody = z.object({
18	  extensionSlug: z.string().min(1),
19	  version: z.string().default("latest"),
20	  // Informational — stored for analytics but not validated against a schema.
21	  agentName: z.string().optional(),
22	  agentVersion: z.string().optional(),
23	});
24	
25	// POST /api/v1/installs
26	// Called by the CLI after a successful local install to record the event.
27	// Requires: Authorization: Bearer <session-token>
28	export async function POST(req: NextRequest) {
29	  const user = await authenticateBearerToken(req);
30	  if (!user) return jsonError("Authentication required.", 401, "unauthenticated");
31	
32	  let body: z.infer<typeof InstallBody>;
33	  try {
34	    body = InstallBody.parse(await req.json());
35	  } catch {
36	    return jsonError("Invalid request body.", 400, "invalid_body");
37	  }
38	
39	  const [ext] = await db
40	    .select({ id: extensions.id, downloadsCount: extensions.downloadsCount })
41	    .from(extensions)
42	    .where(eq(extensions.slug, body.extensionSlug))
43	    .limit(1);
44	
45	  if (!ext) return jsonError("Extension not found.", 404, "not_found");
46	
47	  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(user.id, "installed");
48	  const already = await isInCollection(installedColId, ext.id);
49	
50	  if (!already) {
51	    await db.insert(installs).values({
52	      id: crypto.randomUUID(),
53	      userId: user.id,
54	      extensionId: ext.id,
55	      version: body.version,
56	      source: "cli",
57	    });
58	
59	    await upsertCollectionItem(installedColId, ext.id);
60	
61	    await db
62	      .update(extensions)
63	      .set({ downloadsCount: ext.downloadsCount + 1 })
64	      .where(eq(extensions.id, ext.id));
65	  }
66	
67	  return Response.json({ ok: true, alreadyInstalled: already });
68	}
69

1	import { eq } from "drizzle-orm";
2	import { unzipSync } from "fflate";
3	import { parse } from "smol-toml";
4	import { z } from "zod";
5	
6	import { inngest } from "./client";
7	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
8	import { extensionVersions, files } from "@/lib/db/schema";
9	import { generatePresignedGetUrl } from "@/lib/storage/r2";
10	
11	const BundleManifestSchema = z.object({
12	  extension: z.object({
13	    slug: z.string().min(1).max(64),
14	    name: z.string().min(1),
15	    version: z.string().regex(/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/),
16	    category: z.enum(["skills", "mcp", "slash", "plugins"]),
17	    scope: z.enum(["personal", "org", "enterprise"]),
18	    description: z.string().max(280),
19	  }),
20	  categorization: z.object({
21	    funcCat: z.enum(["workTask", "business", "tools"]),
22	    subCat: z.string().min(1),
23	    l2: z.string().optional(),
24	  }),
25	});
26	
27	export const scanBundle = inngest.createFunction(
28	  {
29	    id: "scan-bundle",
30	    triggers: [{ event: "extension/scan.requested" }],
31	  },
32	  async ({ event, step }) => {
33	    const { versionId, fileId } = event.data as { versionId: string; fileId: string };
34	
35	    // Steps 1–4: fetch, download, checksum, and manifest parsing in one step
36	    // to avoid Buffer serialization issues across step boundaries.
37	    const scanResult = await step.run("download-and-scan", async () => {
38	      const [fileRow] = await db.select().from(files).where(eq(files.id, fileId)).limit(1);
39	      if (!fileRow) return { ok: false as const, reason: "file_not_found", checksum: "" };
40	
41	      const signedUrl = await generatePresignedGetUrl(fileRow.r2Key, 120);
42	      const res = await fetch(signedUrl);
43	      if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`R2 fetch failed: ${res.status}`);
44	      const arrayBuf = await res.arrayBuffer();
45	      const uint8 = new Uint8Array(arrayBuf);
46	
47	      // SHA-256 checksum
48	      const hashBuf = await crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", uint8);
49	      const checksum = Array.from(new Uint8Array(hashBuf))
50	        .map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0"))
51	        .join("");
52	
53	      // Parse manifest.toml
54	      const entries = unzipSync(uint8);
55	      const manifestEntry = entries["manifest.toml"];
56	      if (!manifestEntry) return { ok: false as const, reason: "missing_manifest", checksum };
57	
58	      let raw: unknown;
59	      try {
60	        raw = parse(new TextDecoder().decode(manifestEntry));
61	      } catch {
62	        return { ok: false as const, reason: "invalid_toml", checksum };
63	      }
64	
65	      const parsed = BundleManifestSchema.safeParse(raw);
66	      if (!parsed.success) {
67	        return { ok: false as const, reason: parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ?? "schema_error", checksum };
68	      }
69	
70	      return { ok: true as const, checksum };
71	    });
72	
73	    const scanReport = {
74	      manifestOk: scanResult.ok,
75	      reason: scanResult.ok ? null : scanResult.reason,
76	      checksum: scanResult.checksum,
77	      scannedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
78	    };
79	
80	    if (!scanResult.ok) {
81	      await step.run("mark-rejected", async () => {
82	        await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
83	          await tx.update(files).set({ scanStatus: "flagged", scanReport }).where(eq(files.id, fileId));
84	          await tx.update(extensionVersions).set({ status: "rejected" }).where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));
85	        });
86	      });
87	      return { ok: false, reason: scanResult.reason };
88	    }
89	
90	    await step.run("mark-clean", async () => {
91	      await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
92	        await tx
93	          .update(files)
94	          .set({ scanStatus: "clean", scanReport, checksumSha256: scanResult.checksum })
95	          .where(eq(files.id, fileId));
96	        await tx.update(extensionVersions).set({ status: "ready" }).where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));
97	      });
98	    });
99	
100	    await step.sendEvent("enqueue-index", {
101	      name: "extension/index.requested",
102	      data: { versionId },
103	    });
104	
105	    return { ok: true, checksum: scanResult.checksum };
106	  },
107	);
108	
```

I verified both duplication claims. Quick note: there's no `CONTEXT.md` and no `docs/adr/` yet, so I'm working from `docs/architecture.md` for domain vocabulary (Extension, Version, Bundle, Install, Filter, Manifest).

# Deepening candidates

## 1. Install recording — duplicated module, two near-identical implementations

**Files**
- `lib/actions/install.ts:25-55` (web)
- `app/api/v1/installs/route.ts:39-65` (CLI)

**Problem**
Both sites repeat the same four-step recipe: lookup extension → `getOrCreateSystemCollection(user, "installed")` → `isInCollection` check → insert into `installs` + `upsertCollectionItem` + bump `downloadsCount`. The only real differences are: (a) lookup by `id` vs `slug`, (b) `source: "web"` vs `"cli"`. They've already drifted — the web action increments atomically with `sql\`...+ 1\``, the CLI route does a read-then-write `ext.downloadsCount + 1` (lib/actions/install.ts:53 vs app/api/v1/installs/route.ts:63). That's a concurrency bug that exists *only because* the logic is duplicated.

**Solution**
A single Install module: `recordInstall(userId, extensionRef, source, version)` where `extensionRef` accepts `{ id }` or `{ slug }`. It owns the system-collection lookup, the dedupe check, the counter bump, and the source label. Web action and CLI route shrink to thin authentication-and-shape wrappers.

**Benefits**
- Locality: the rule "an install means insert + collection upsert + counter bump" lives once. Future changes (e.g. quotas, idempotency keys, install-event publishing) edit one file.
- Leverage: callers get back `{ alreadyInstalled }` from a one-line call and stop knowing about `installs` / `collections` / `downloadsCount`.
- The interface is the test surface — today there are no install tests because the surface is split across a server action and an HTTP route. One module, one set of tests covering both surfaces.

---

## 2. Version lifecycle — `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` writes to four tables inline

**Files**
- `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts:80-98` (mark-rejected, mark-clean step bodies)
- `lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts` (parallel transitions for the publish step)
- `lib/actions/publish.ts` (sets initial draft state)

**Problem**
The Inngest job knows the schema of `files`, `extensionVersions`, *and* (in reindex-search) `extensions` plus `search_vector`. Two `step.run` blocks in `scan-bundle.ts` each open a transaction and update `files.scanStatus` + `extensionVersions.status` together. The **invariant** ("a version is `ready` iff its file is `clean` with a recorded checksum") is enforced by every caller, not by the schema or a module — so a fourth job that wants to flip a version's status has to re-learn the column set.

**Solution**
A Version-state module — `lib/extension-state/version.ts` — exposing transitions: `markVersionRejected(versionId, reason, scanReport)`, `markVersionReady(versionId, fileId, checksum, scanReport)`, `publishVersion(versionId, searchVector)`. Each function owns the tables it touches and the transaction. Jobs orchestrate (`step.run("mark-rejected", () => markVersionRejected(...))`); they don't issue Drizzle calls.

**Benefits**
- Locality: the column set involved in "rejecting a version" is in one file. If we later add a `rejectedAt` timestamp or a `scan_failures` row, one edit.
- Leverage: `reindex-search.ts` and `scan-bundle.ts` (and any future job — `republish`, `unpublish`, `republish-after-edit`) call the same transitions and get the same invariants.
- Tests get a real seam: today, testing the scan logic forces you to fake Inngest's `step.run`. A version-state module is plain async functions you can hit with a real test DB.

---

## 3. Manifest schema — two zod schemas describing overlapping shapes

**Files**
- `lib/validators/manifest.ts:3-27` (`ManifestFormSchema` — flat, used by the publish form)
- `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts:11-25` (`BundleManifestSchema` — nested `[extension]` + `[categorization]`, used to validate `manifest.toml`)

**Problem**
Both schemas describe "what makes an extension manifest valid": same `slug`, `name`, `version`, `category`, `scope`, `funcCat`, `subCat`, `l2`. They've already drifted (form: `slug` regex + length ≤ 80; bundle: ≤ 64 with no regex; form: `description ≤ 300`; bundle: `≤ 280`). When a user uploads a bundle whose `manifest.toml` would have failed the form schema, today's code happily accepts it.

This one is more nuanced than a flat extract — the form has UI-only fields (`tagIds`, `deptId`, `descriptionZh`, URL fields) that don't belong on disk, and the bundle is grouped by TOML section. So it's not "merge into one schema" — it's "the *shared core* is one schema, and form/bundle each compose it with their context-specific extras."

**Solution**
`lib/validators/extension-manifest.ts` defines a single `ExtensionManifestCore` schema for the shared fields. `ManifestFormSchema` extends it with UI fields. The bundle parser reads the TOML, lifts `[extension]` + `[categorization]` into one flat object, then validates against the same core (plus any bundle-only constraints).

**Benefits**
- Locality: rules like "slug is `/^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/`, max 64 chars" are written once.
- Leverage: future tightening (a new required field, a stricter regex) automatically flows to both surfaces. Tests on the core cover both.

---

## 4. Filter — semantics of "what filters mean" spread across four files

**Files**
- `lib/validators/filters.ts` (parse + validate URL params)
- `lib/search/query.ts` (`buildExtensionWhere`, `buildExtensionOrder`)
- `lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts` (client-side URL ↔ state)
- `components/filters/*` (every island reaches into the URL directly via the hook)

**Problem**
The `Filters` concept is genuinely a thing — it parses, validates, persists in the URL, and turns into SQL. But its definition is fragmented: shape lives in the validator, SQL projection lives in `search/query.ts`, URL projection lives in `hooks/use-filter-url.ts`. A change like "add a `stars` minimum filter" touches at minimum: filters validator, query builder, the hook, and the component that exposes the chip.

**Solution**
`lib/filters/index.ts` becomes the home of the Filters concept: the schema, `parseFromUrl`, `toSqlWhere`, `toSqlOrder`, plus a `useFilters()` hook that returns a typed `Filters` object and a setter (built on top of the URL). Today's `parseFilters` and the SQL builders become internal details.

**Benefits**
- Locality: adding a filter is one file plus one chip component. No more "did I update all four places?"
- Leverage: components stop knowing about `searchParams` or how the URL is encoded; tests can construct a `Filters` literal and assert against the SQL or the URL output without rendering.

This is the lower-stakes candidate of the four — the current split is annoying but not buggy. Worth doing only if filters are likely to grow.

---

## Not candidates (already deep, or hypothetical seams)

- **`lib/storage/r2.ts`** — already deep. Three small functions hide the AWS SDK, presigning, key composition, and credentials. Deleting it concentrates complexity. Leave it.
- **`cli/src/installers/`** — currently a one-adapter "polymorphic" dispatcher. Hypothetical seam, but it's a *placeholder* for distinct install logic per category (mcp, plugins, slash). Don't deepen until a second adapter actually exists.
- **`lib/data/*` + `lib/db/queries/*`** — the explore agent flagged these as a shallow "data layer." After looking, I disagree: `lib/data/` is mock seed data (will be deleted, not refactored), and `lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` already collapses non-trivial logic (joins, search-vector ranking). Folding visibility/scope rules in there would help, but it's a smaller win than candidates 1–3.

---

**Which would you like to grill on?** My ranking:

1. **Install recording** — strongest case (real bug already, real duplication, small surface).
2. **Version lifecycle** — high payoff for testability of the publish flow.
3. **Manifest schema core** — easy to underestimate; the drift is silent.
4. **Filter concept** — defensible but speculative until filters actually grow.

> DEVELOPER

1	import { and, eq } from "drizzle-orm";
2	
3	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
4	import { collectionItems, collections } from "@/lib/db/schema/collection";
5	
6	export async function getOrCreateSystemCollection(
7	  userId: string,
8	  kind: "installed" | "saved",
9	): Promise<string> {
10	  const existing = await db
11	    .select({ id: collections.id })
12	    .from(collections)
13	    .where(
14	      and(eq(collections.ownerUserId, userId), eq(collections.systemKind, kind)),
15	    )
16	    .limit(1);
17	
18	  if (existing[0]) return existing[0].id;
19	
20	  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
21	  await db.insert(collections).values({
22	    id,
23	    ownerUserId: userId,
24	    name: kind === "installed" ? "Installed" : "Saved",
25	    systemKind: kind,
26	  });
27	  return id;
28	}
29	
30	export async function isInCollection(
31	  collectionId: string,
32	  extensionId: string,
33	): Promise<boolean> {
34	  const row = await db
35	    .select({ collectionId: collectionItems.collectionId })
36	    .from(collectionItems)
37	    .where(
38	      and(
39	        eq(collectionItems.collectionId, collectionId),
40	        eq(collectionItems.extensionId, extensionId),
41	      ),
42	    )
43	    .limit(1);
44	  return row.length > 0;
45	}
46	
47	export async function upsertCollectionItem(
48	  collectionId: string,
49	  extensionId: string,
50	) {
51	  await db
52	    .insert(collectionItems)
53	    .values({ collectionId, extensionId })
54	    .onConflictDoNothing();
55	}
56	
57	export async function getUserCollections(userId: string) {
58	  return db
59	    .select({
60	      id: collections.id,
61	      name: collections.name,
62	      systemKind: collections.systemKind,
63	    })
64	    .from(collections)
65	    .where(eq(collections.ownerUserId, userId))
66	    .orderBy(collections.createdAt);
67	}

> AGENT

Before I sketch the interface, three load-bearing questions. Each one branches the design.

### Q1. What does an install record actually mean?

The schema and the code disagree right now. The schema says:
- `installs.version` is **`notNull`** with no default (lib/db/schema/activity.ts:26)
- only a **non-unique index** on `(userId, extensionId)` — multiple rows per (user, ext) are allowed
- there's a `uninstalledAt` column, suggesting installs are individual lifetime events

But the code says:
- web records `version: "latest"` always (lib/actions/install.ts:44)
- both surfaces dedupe via the `installed` system collection — so a given (user, ext) never gets a second `installs` row, no matter how many times they reinstall or upgrade

So which is it?

- **(a) "User X has this extension."** Dedupe on (user, ext). One row, ever. `version` should probably go away or become "first installed version." `uninstalledAt` is doing real work. `downloadsCount` = distinct users.
- **(b) "User X obtained extension Y at version V."** No dedupe. Every CLI install / version upgrade writes a row. `downloadsCount` = total install events. The collection is a separate concept ("currently installed" — derived from latest non-uninstalled row).
- **(c) Something else** — e.g. dedupe on (user, ext, version), so reinstalling the same version is a no-op but upgrading writes a new row.

This decision dictates the module's interface. (a) is what the code currently does; (b) is what the schema looks built for. The web hardcoding `"latest"` is a smell either way.

### Q2. Where does the module live?

The wrappers can't share a path:
- `lib/actions/install.ts` is `"use server"` — Next-flavored server action, only callable from RSCs/forms
- `app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` is a Node HTTP handler

The shared module needs a neutral home. Options:
- **(a) `lib/installs/record.ts`** — sibling to `lib/storage/`, `lib/auth/`, `lib/jobs/`. Plain async fn. Both wrappers import it. (My default.)
- **(b) `lib/db/queries/installs.ts`** — but this isn't a query; it's a multi-table transaction with policy. Wrong drawer.
- **(c) Keep `lib/actions/install.ts`, make the API route call the action.** Server actions *can* be called from route handlers in Next 15+, but the call goes through the server-action runtime, which is heavier than a plain function call and less testable.

Default = (a). Push back if you have a reason.

### Q3. Is the install lookup the module's job, or the caller's?

Today web takes `extensionId`, CLI takes `extensionSlug`. The lookup is part of what's duplicated.

- **(a)** Module accepts `{ id }` | `{ slug }` (discriminated union), owns the lookup. One call site = one round-trip.
- **(b)** Module takes `extensionId` only. Wrappers resolve slug→id first. The lookup module isn't strictly an install concern.
- **(c)** Module takes `extensionId` and an optional `expectedSlug` for the CLI case (defends against the slug having moved between the CLI's metadata fetch and the install POST).

The CLI route already does a `select` for `id` + `downloadsCount` because it can't trust the caller has the id. The web action already has the id from the click handler. (a) keeps both wrappers truly trivial; (b) leaves the CLI route still doing a lookup. I'd lean (a) — but (c) is interesting if you care about TOCTOU between the CLI's `GET /extensions/:slug` and `POST /installs`.

---

**Pick (a/b/c) for each.** Q1 is the one I most want you to commit on — the rest follow.

> DEVELOPER

Q1: b is good, Q2: a is good, Q3: a

> AGENT

Locked: every call writes a row, module lives at `lib/installs/record.ts`, owns the lookup.

A consequence to flag before going further: under (b), `downloadsCount` redefines from "distinct users who installed" to "total install events." Existing rows were computed under the old rule. Mostly cosmetic for a small dataset, but worth noting — if you want to preserve the "unique users" metric, it becomes a `COUNT(DISTINCT user_id)` view or a separate column. Flagging, not blocking.

Three more questions.

### Q4. What does the module return?

Today it's `{ ok, alreadyInstalled }`. Under (b), `alreadyInstalled` no longer maps to "I no-op'd" because every call inserts. UI still cares — the web button wants to know whether to say "Install" vs "Reinstall"; the CLI may want to print "(already had this)".

- **(a) `{ installId, isFirstInstall, version }`** — leaves messaging to callers. `isFirstInstall = SELECT count() = 0 prior to insert`. Lean.
- **(b) `{ installId, alreadyInCollection }`** — names the UI concern directly. Same query, different word.
- **(c) Just `{ installId }`** — minimum surface. Callers who need UI flavor query collections themselves.

(a) is my default — `isFirstInstall` is the cleanest concept ("has this user ever obtained this ext before?") and decouples from collection membership, which is now a separate idea.

### Q5. The `version: "latest"` problem

Under (b), web is about to start writing literal `"latest"` strings into a column whose CLI peers contain `"1.2.3"`. That's noise. Three ways to fix:

- **(a)** Module signature is `version: string`. Web wrapper resolves "latest" → current semver before calling. (Web wrapper now needs to know which row holds the current version.)
- **(b)** Module signature is `version: string | "latest"`, resolves "latest" internally by reading the extension's current published version. The module owns the "latest" concept.
- **(c)** Module signature is `{ version?: string }` — undefined means "current latest." Module always resolves to a real semver before insert. CLI passes its real version; web omits it. The string `"latest"` never enters the column.

(c) is my default — turns "latest" into the *absence* of a pin rather than a sentinel string. The column is always a real semver.

This does mean the module needs to know how to find the latest version of an extension. Today there's a `currentVersionId` on the `extensions` row (I'd guess — let me verify if you confirm this direction). If that exists, it's one extra read; if not, this is a meatier dependency than it looks.

### Q6. Transaction?

Today neither path uses one. Three writes per call: `installs` insert, `collectionItems` upsert, `extensions.downloadsCount` increment. Under (b), every call exercises all three. If write 2 or 3 fails, you have an install row but no collection entry / wrong counter.

- **(a)** Wrap in `db.transaction(...)`. All-or-nothing. Costs one extra round-trip; gives atomic semantics.
- **(b)** No transaction. The install row is the source of truth. Counter and collection drift becomes possible but visible (counter and `COUNT(installs)` would diverge).
- **(c)** Compromise: counter uses atomic `sql\`+ 1\``, the install + collection writes go in a transaction, counter goes outside. Slightly weird but it's what the web action *almost* does today.

(a) is my default. The counter divergence in (b) is exactly the kind of subtle drift that's painful to debug six months in.

---

**Pick (a/b/c) for each.** And confirm or correct: does `extensions` have a `currentVersionId` column for Q5? If not, what's the source-of-truth for "the current published version"?

> DEVELOPER

Q4: a; Q5: c Q6: a

> AGENT

Confirmed: there's no `currentVersionId` on `extensions` — `extension_versions` has a unique `(extensionId, version)` and a `publishedAt` timestamp, plus `status = 'ready'`. So "latest" is a derived concept, and it's not free.

Two more questions, then I'll write the signature.

### Q7. What does "latest" mean?

The module needs to resolve `version: undefined` → a real semver. Two reasonable definitions:

- **(a) Most recently published** — `WHERE status='ready' ORDER BY publishedAt DESC LIMIT 1`. Simple, matches what most registries do, no semver-parsing dependency.
- **(b) Highest semver** — ranks `1.10.0` above `1.2.0`. Lexicographic sort breaks. Needs a `semver`-aware order, either in JS (after fetching all ready rows) or via a Postgres semver function.
- **(c) Most recently published, but require it pass a "newer or equal to current readme version" check** — overkill for now.

(a) is the registry default (npm, crates.io). It also matches "what got published last," which is what publishers expect. (b) is what users *think* they want until they realize a publisher pulled a broken release and "latest" should now mean the rollback, not the highest semver.

I'd lock (a). Same answer the rest of the codebase uses if/when it surfaces this concept — worth keeping consistent.

### Q8. Error model

Today the web action returns `{ ok: false, error: "unauthenticated" | "not_found" | "server_error" }`; the CLI route returns HTTP errors via `jsonError`. The shared module shouldn't handle auth (wrappers do). What does it return on failure?

- **(a) Throw on unrecoverable errors, return only the success shape.** `recordInstall(...): Promise<{ installId, isFirstInstall, version }>`. Throws a typed `InstallError` with `code: "extension_not_found" | "no_published_version"`. Wrappers catch and translate to their surface (HTTP status, action error).
- **(b) Return a discriminated union** `{ ok: true, ... } | { ok: false, code: "..." }`. Mirrors today's action shape.
- **(c) Throw for *truly* exceptional (db down), return the union for *expected* failure modes (not_found).** The "expected vs exceptional" split.

I'd lock (a) — the module's job is to *do* the install. Lookup failures and "no published version" are exceptional from the module's perspective; they shouldn't share a shape with the success path. Wrappers know their surface and translate. (Plus: `installId` is non-null only on success, which the union models awkwardly anyway.)

But this is a project-style call. If the rest of `lib/` returns `{ ok, error }` unions, locality says match. Quick check would settle it — looking at `lib/actions/install.ts` and `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts`, the codebase mixes both: actions tend to use unions (because Next surfaces them nicely to forms), but `lib/db/queries/*` and `lib/jobs/*` throw. The new module is closer to the latter group — it's not a Next action — so (a) is consistent.

---

**Pick (a/b/c) for each, or push back.** After this, I'll write the signature + the wrapper diffs, you sign off, then we implement.

> AGENT

Locked. Before I write the diff, one UX consequence of (b) I want you to look at, because it changes web behavior in a way the wrapper alone won't fix.

### A consequence to flag

Under the old code, clicking "Install" twice in a row was a no-op the second time (dedupe via the `installed` collection). Under (b), **every click writes a row and bumps the counter**. A user double-clicking the button adds 2 to `downloadsCount`. The current `InstallButton` component (`components/extension/install-button.tsx`) needs at minimum a debounce, and probably should flip its label to "Reinstall" once `isFirstInstall === false` so the second click is intentional.

Two options:
- **(i)** Just debounce / disable while pending. Treat the second click as user intent if it lands.
- **(ii)** Keep the *web* surface idempotent — module always records, but the web button checks "already installed in collection?" first and shows "Reinstall" requiring an explicit second click.

(i) is what every package registry does. The "Install" → "Installed" → click again → records reinstall pattern. (ii) is overkill.

I'll go with (i) unless you push back. Web button shows the spinner on click, disables until the action returns, then shows "Installed ✓" with `isFirstInstall ? "Installed" : "Reinstalled"` flash.

### Final design

```ts
// lib/installs/record.ts

import { and, desc, eq, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
import { extensions, extensionVersions } from "@/lib/db/schema/extension";
import { installs } from "@/lib/db/schema/activity";
import { collectionItems } from "@/lib/db/schema/collection";
import { getOrCreateSystemCollection } from "@/lib/db/queries/collections";

export type ExtensionRef = { id: string } | { slug: string };
export type InstallSource = "web" | "cli";

export class InstallError extends Error {
  constructor(public code: "extension_not_found" | "no_published_version") {
    super(code);
    this.name = "InstallError";
  }
}

export async function recordInstall(params: {
  userId: string;
  extension: ExtensionRef;
  source: InstallSource;
  version?: string; // undefined → latest published
}): Promise<{ installId: string; isFirstInstall: boolean; version: string }> {
  // 1. Resolve ref → id
  // 2. Resolve version (undefined → latest ready, ORDER BY publishedAt DESC)
  // 3. Pre-resolve "installed" collection id (idempotent)
  // 4. db.transaction:
  //    - read prior install for (user, ext) → isFirstInstall
  //    - insert installs
  //    - insert collectionItems ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
  //    - update extensions.downloadsCount = downloadsCount + 1 (atomic sql`...`)
  //    - return { installId, isFirstInstall, version }
}
```

Two implementation notes:

- **Collection bootstrap stays outside the tx.** `getOrCreateSystemCollection` uses the global `db`, not a tx handle. Refactoring it to accept an optional tx is a wider change. Resolving the id *before* the tx is fine: it's a pure read after the first ever install, and there's no unique constraint on `(ownerUserId, systemKind)` anyway, so pulling it inside the tx wouldn't actually defend against the race.
- **`isFirstInstall` is a SELECT inside the tx, before the INSERT.** Postgres default isolation (READ COMMITTED) means a concurrent first-install by the same user could both observe zero priors. In practice impossible (one user, one button) but worth noting for correctness — if you ever care, change to `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` or check via a unique constraint trick. Not blocking.

### Wrapper diffs (sketch only — no code yet)

**`lib/actions/install.ts`** — shrinks to: get session → call `recordInstall({ userId, extension: { id }, source: "web" })` → catch `InstallError` and translate to the discriminated union the form needs. Return shape changes from `{ alreadyInstalled }` to `{ installId, isFirstInstall, version }`. **Calls into `InstallButton` need updating** (and its test).

**`app/api/v1/installs/route.ts`** — shrinks to: bearer auth → zod parse body → translate `version === "latest" ? undefined : body.version` → call `recordInstall({ userId, extension: { slug }, source: "cli", version })` → catch `InstallError` → 404 for `extension_not_found`, 422 for `no_published_version`. Response body changes shape. **No CLI client change needed** — the CLI sends what it sends; the response is currently only checked for `ok`.

### Existing data

A few `installs` rows already have `version = "latest"` from past web installs. Not blocking — new rows will be clean. If you want, a one-shot SQL: `UPDATE installs SET version = (SELECT v.version FROM extension_versions v WHERE v.extension_id = installs.extension_id AND v.status = 'ready' ORDER BY v.published_at DESC LIMIT 1) WHERE version = 'latest';` — runs once after deploy, fine to skip.

### Tests

Recommend integration tests against a real DB (per project convention — bun:test, the existing query tests do this):
- first install: row inserted, counter = 1, isFirstInstall = true, in collection
- reinstall same version: row inserted, counter = 2, isFirstInstall = false, collection unchanged (already in)
- upgrade: passes `version = "1.2.0"` explicitly, row records that version
- omitted version: resolves to latest published
- omitted version, no published versions exist: throws `InstallError("no_published_version")`
- bad slug / id: throws `InstallError("extension_not_found")`

The web action and CLI route then get one thin test each — they exist to translate, not to compute.

---

**Sign off?** If this looks right, I'll:
1. Write `lib/installs/record.ts` + tests
2. Shrink `lib/actions/install.ts`
3. Shrink `app/api/v1/installs/route.ts`
4. Update `InstallButton` for the new return shape + debounce
5. Open as one branch / one PR per project convention.

Or push back on any piece. The UX consequence (i vs ii) is the only open call.

3	import { Check, Download } from "lucide-react";
4	import { useTranslations } from "next-intl";
5	import { useState, useTransition } from "react";
6	
7	import { installExtension } from "@/lib/actions/install";
8	import { useRouter } from "@/lib/i18n/navigation";
9	
10	type Size = "sm" | "lg";
11	
12	interface InstallButtonProps {
13	  extensionId: string;
14	  size?: Size;
15	}
16	
17	export function InstallButton({
18	  extensionId,
19	  size = "sm",
20	}: InstallButtonProps) {
21	  const t = useTranslations("common");
22	  const router = useRouter();
23	  const [isPending, startTransition] = useTransition();
24	  const [installed, setInstalled] = useState(false);
25	
26	  async function handleClick() {
27	    startTransition(async () => {
28	      const result = await installExtension(extensionId);
29	
30	      if (!result.ok && result.error === "unauthenticated") {
31	        router.push("/sign-in");
32	        return;
33	      }
34	
35	      if (result.ok) {
36	        setInstalled(true);
37	        // Reset after 3 s so the user can see the confirmation.
38	        setTimeout(() => setInstalled(false), 3000);
39	      }
40	    });
41	  }
42	
43	  if (size === "lg") {
44	    return (
45	      <button
46	        type="button"
47	        onClick={handleClick}
48	        disabled={isPending || installed}
49	        className="bg-primary text-primary-foreground hover:opacity-90 inline-flex shrink-0 items-center gap-1.5 rounded-md px-4 py-2 text-[13px] font-bold transition disabled:opacity-70"
50	      >
51	        {installed ? (
52	          <Check className="size-3.5" />
53	        ) : (
54	          <Download className="size-3.5" />
55	        )}
56	        {isPending
57	          ? t("installing")
58	          : installed
59	            ? t("installed")
60	            : t("install")}
61	      </button>
62	    );
63	  }
64	
65	  return (
66	    <button
67	      type="button"
68	      onClick={handleClick}
69	      disabled={isPending || installed}
70	      className="bg-primary text-primary-foreground flex items-center gap-1 rounded-md px-3 py-1 text-[12px] font-bold transition hover:opacity-90 disabled:opacity-70"
71	    >
72	      {installed ? (
73	        <Check className="size-3" />
74	      ) : (
75	        <Download className="size-3" />
76	      )}
77	      {isPending
78	        ? t("installing")
79	        : installed
80	          ? t("installed")
81	          : t("install")}
82	    </button>
83	  );
84	}
85

1	import { Command } from "commander";
2	import { fetchExtension, getBundleUrl, postInstallEvent } from "../api";
3	import { installExtension } from "../installers";
4	
5	export function makeInstallCommand(): Command {
6	  return new Command("install")
7	    .description("Install an extension by slug")
8	    .argument("<slug>", "Extension slug (e.g. acme/my-skill)")
9	    .option("--dry-run", "Resolve and show what would be installed without writing files")
10	    .action(async (slug: string, opts: { dryRun?: boolean }) => {
11	      let ext;
12	      try {
13	        ext = await fetchExtension(slug);
14	      } catch (err) {
15	        console.error((err as Error).message);
16	        process.exit(1);
17	      }
18	
19	      console.log(`\n${ext.name} ${ext.version}`);
20	      if (ext.description) console.log(`  ${ext.description}`);
21	
22	      let bundleUrl: string;
23	      try {
24	        bundleUrl = await getBundleUrl(slug);
25	      } catch (err) {
26	        const msg = (err as Error).message;
27	        if (msg.toLowerCase().includes("not available")) {
28	          console.error(`Bundle not yet available for "${slug}". Check back later.`);
29	        } else {
30	          console.error(`Failed to fetch bundle: ${msg}`);
31	        }
32	        process.exit(1);
33	      }
34	
35	      if (opts.dryRun) {
36	        console.log(`\n[dry-run] Would download: ${bundleUrl}`);
37	        console.log(`[dry-run] Category: ${ext.category}`);
38	        return;
39	      }
40	
41	      process.stdout.write("Downloading...");
42	      let zipBuffer: ArrayBuffer;
43	      try {
44	        const res = await fetch(bundleUrl);
45	        if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);
46	        zipBuffer = await res.arrayBuffer();
47	        process.stdout.write(" done\n");
48	      } catch (err) {
49	        process.stdout.write("\n");
50	        console.error(`Download failed: ${(err as Error).message}`);
51	        process.exit(1);
52	      }
53	
54	      let destDir: string;
55	      try {
56	        destDir = await installExtension(slug, ext.category, zipBuffer);
57	      } catch (err) {
58	        console.error(`Install failed: ${(err as Error).message}`);
59	        process.exit(1);
60	      }
61	
62	      await postInstallEvent(slug, ext.version).catch(() => {});
63	
64	      console.log(`Installed to ${destDir}`);
65	    });
66	}
67	
```

> AGENT

Confirmed: tests mock `@/lib/db/client` rather than hit a real DB. I'll match that pattern. Also: transactions work in this project (scan-bundle.ts uses them) but neon-http's transaction is non-interactive — reads can't feed into writes inside the same tx. I'll pre-resolve everything and put only the three atomic writes inside the transaction. Race window for concurrent same-user first-installs already exists at the isolation level, so pulling the check outside doesn't add risk.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/installs/record.ts",
  "content": "import { and, desc, eq, sql } from \"drizzle-orm\";\n\nimport { db } from \"@/lib/db/client\";\nimport { installs } from \"@/lib/db/schema/activity\";\nimport { collectionItems } from \"@/lib/db/schema/collection\";\nimport { extensions, extensionVersions } from \"@/lib/db/schema/extension\";\nimport { getOrCreateSystemCollection } from \"@/lib/db/queries/collections\";\n\nexport type ExtensionRef = { id: string } | { slug: string };\nexport type InstallSource = \"web\" | \"cli\";\n\nexport type InstallErrorCode = \"extension_not_found\" | \"no_published_version\";\n\nexport class InstallError extends Error {\n  readonly code: InstallErrorCode;\n  constructor(code: InstallErrorCode) {\n    super(code);\n    this.code = code;\n    this.name = \"InstallError\";\n  }\n}\n\nexport interface RecordInstallParams {\n  userId: string;\n  extension: ExtensionRef;\n  source: InstallSource;\n  version?: string;\n}\n\nexport interface InstallRecord {\n  installId: string;\n  isFirstInstall: boolean;\n  version: string;\n}\n\nexport async function recordInstall(\n  params: RecordInstallParams,\n): Promise<InstallRecord> {\n  const { userId, extension, source } = params;\n\n  const extWhere =\n    \"id\" in extension\n      ? eq(extensions.id, extension.id)\n      : eq(extensions.slug, extension.slug);\n\n  const [ext] = await db\n    .select({ id: extensions.id })\n    .from(extensions)\n    .where(extWhere)\n    .limit(1);\n\n  if (!ext) throw new InstallError(\"extension_not_found\");\n  const extensionId = ext.id;\n\n  let version = params.version;\n  if (!version) {\n    const [latest] = await db\n      .select({ version: extensionVersions.version })\n      .from(extensionVersions)\n      .where(\n        and(\n          eq(extensionVersions.extensionId, extensionId),\n          eq(extensionVersions.status, \"ready\"),\n        ),\n      )\n      .orderBy(desc(extensionVersions.publishedAt))\n      .limit(1);\n    if (!latest) throw new InstallError(\"no_published_version\");\n    version = latest.version;\n  }\n\n  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(userId, \"installed\");\n\n  const prior = await db\n    .select({ id: installs.id })\n    .from(installs)\n    .where(and(eq(installs.userId, userId), eq(installs.extensionId, extensionId)))\n    .limit(1);\n  const isFirstInstall = prior.length === 0;\n\n  const installId = crypto.randomUUID();\n\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    await tx.insert(installs).values({\n      id: installId,\n      userId,\n      extensionId,\n      version: version!,\n      source,\n    });\n\n    await tx\n      .insert(collectionItems)\n      .values({ collectionId: installedColId, extensionId })\n      .onConflictDoNothing();\n\n    await tx\n      .update(extensions)\n      .set({ downloadsCount: sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} + 1` })\n      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n  });\n\n  return { installId, isFirstInstall, version };\n}\n"
}

callback.\n// Returns a fake tx object that records what was written.\nfunction makeTx() {\n  const insertValues = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({\n    onConflictDoNothing: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),\n  });\n  const insertValuesPlain = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n  const insert = vi.fn().mockImplementation(() => ({\n    // installs insert: just .values() resolves\n    // collectionItems insert: .values().onConflictDoNothing() resolves\n    // We return both shapes via a thenable-ish object.\n    values: vi.fn().mockImplementation((row: unknown) => {\n      // If row has `collectionId`, it's the collectionItems insert.\n      if (row && typeof row === \"object\" && \"collectionId\" in row) {\n        return { onConflictDoNothing: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined) };\n      }\n      return Promise.resolve(undefined);\n    }),\n  }));\n  const update = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({\n    set: vi.fn().mockReturnValue({\n      where: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),\n    }),\n  });\n  return { insert, update, _insertValues: insertValues, _insertValuesPlain: insertValuesPlain };\n}\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n  // Default: transaction runs the callback with a fresh fake tx.\n  transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) => {\n    return cb(makeTx());\n  });\n  // Default: top-level insert/update resolve to nothing (not used in happy path).\n  insertMock.mockReturnValue({\n    values: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),\n  });\n  updateMock.mockReturnValue({\n    set: vi.fn().mockReturnValue({\n      where: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),\n    }),\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"recordInstall\", () => {\n  it(\"throws extension_not_found when lookup by id misses\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([])); // ext lookup empty\n\n    await expect(\n      recordInstall({\n        userId: \"u1\",\n        extension: { id: \"missing\" },\n        source: \"web\",\n      }),\n    ).rejects.toThrow(InstallError);\n    await expect(\n      recordInstall({\n        userId: \"u1\",\n        extension: { id: \"missing\" },\n        source: \"web\",\n      }).catch((e) => e.code),\n    ).resolves.toBe(\"extension_not_found\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws extension_not_found when lookup by slug misses\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([]));\n    await expect(\n      recordInstall({\n        userId: \"u1\",\n        extension: { slug: \"missing\" },\n        source: \"cli\",\n      }),\n    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: \"extension_not_found\" });\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws no_published_version when version omitted and no ready version exists\", async () => {\n    selectMock\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"ext-1\" }])) // ext lookup\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([])); // version lookup empty\n\n    await expect(\n      recordInstall({\n        userId: \"u1\",\n        extension: { id: \"ext-1\" },\n        source: \"web\",\n      }),\n    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: \"no_published_version\" });\n  });\n\n  it(\"returns success shape on first install with explicit version\", async () => {\n    selectMock\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"ext-1\" }])) // ext lookup\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([])); // prior install lookup (none)\n\n    getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock.mockResolvedValue(\"col-installed\");\n\n    const result = await recordInstall({\n      userId: \"u1\",\n      extension: { id: \"ext-1\" },\n      source: \"web\",\n      version: \"1.2.3\",\n    });\n\n    expect(result).toMatchObject({\n      isFirstInstall: true,\n      version: \"1.2.3\",\n    });\n    expect(result.installId).toBeTypeOf(\"string\");\n    expect(result.installId.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n    expect(transactionMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n  });\n\n  it(\"resolves version from latest published when version omitted\", async () => {\n    selectMock\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"ext-1\" }])) // ext\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ version: \"2.0.0\" }])) // latest\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([])); // prior installs\n\n    getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock.mockResolvedValue(\"col-installed\");\n\n    const result = await recordInstall({\n      userId: \"u1\",\n      extension: { slug: \"my-skill\" },\n      source: \"cli\",\n    });\n\n    expect(result.version).toBe(\"2.0.0\");\n    expect(result.isFirstInstall).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it(\"reports isFirstInstall=false when prior install exists\", async () => {\n    selectMock\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"ext-1\" }])) // ext\n      .mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"prev-install\" }])); // prior\n\n    getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock.mockResolvedValue(\"col-installed\");\n\n    const result = await recordInstall({\n      userId: \"u1\",\n      extension: { id: \"ext-1\" },\n      source: \"cli\",\n {
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/actions/install.ts",
  "content": "\"use server\";\n\nimport { getSession } from \"@/lib/auth/session\";\nimport { InstallError, recordInstall } from \"@/lib/installs/record\";\n\nexport type InstallResult =\n  | {\n      ok: true;\n      installId: string;\n      isFirstInstall: boolean;\n      version: string;\n    }\n  | {\n      ok: false;\n      error:\n        | \"unauthenticated\"\n        | \"not_found\"\n        | \"no_published_version\"\n        | \"server_error\";\n    };\n\nexport async function installExtension(\n  extensionId: string,\n): Promise<InstallResult> {\n  const session = await getSession();\n  if (!session) return { ok: false, error: \"unauthenticated\" };\n\n  try {\n    const result = await recordInstall({\n      userId: session.user.id,\n      extension: { id: extensionId },\n      source: \"web\",\n    });\n    return { ok: true, ...result };\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (e instanceof InstallError) {\n      return {\n        ok: false,\n        error: e.code === \"extension_not_found\" ? \"not_found\" : e.code,\n      };\n    }\n    return { ok: false, error: \"server_error\" };\n  }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/app/api/v1/installs/route.ts",
  "content": "import { NextRequest } from \"next/server\";\nimport { z } from \"zod/v4\";\n\nimport { authenticateBearerToken, jsonError } from \"@/lib/api/auth\";\nimport { InstallError, recordInstall } from \"@/lib/installs/record\";\n\nexport const runtime = \"nodejs\";\n\nconst InstallBody = z.object({\n  extensionSlug: z.string().min(1),\n  // CLI clients historically send \"latest\" as a sentinel; we resolve it to\n  // the actual published version inside recordInstall when omitted.\n  version: z.string().default(\"latest\"),\n  // Informational — stored for analytics but not validated against a schema.\n  agentName: z.string().optional(),\n  agentVersion: z.string().optional(),\n});\n\n// POST /api/v1/installs\n// Called by the CLI after a successful local install to record the event.\n// Requires: Authorization: Bearer <session-token>\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  const user = await authenticateBearerToken(req);\n  if (!user) return jsonError(\"Authentication required.\", 401, \"unauthenticated\");\n\n  let body: z.infer<typeof InstallBody>;\n  try {\n    body = InstallBody.parse(await req.json());\n  } catch {\n    return jsonError(\"Invalid request body.\", 400, \"invalid_body\");\n  }\n\n  const version = body.version === \"latest\" ? undefined : body.version;\n\n  try {\n    const result = await recordInstall({\n      userId: user.id,\n      extension: { slug: body.extensionSlug },\n      source: \"cli\",\n      version,\n    });\n    return Response.json({ ok: true, ...result });\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (e instanceof InstallError) {\n      const status = e.code === \"extension_not_found\" ? 404 : 422;\n      return jsonError(e.code, status, e.code);\n    }\n    throw e;\n  }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

All green: lint, typecheck, 134 tests pass.

## Summary of changes

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `lib/installs/record.ts` | **new** — single deep module that owns: extension lookup (id or slug), version resolution (latest published), install row insert, collection upsert, atomic counter bump. Throws typed `InstallError`. |
| `lib/installs/record.test.ts` | **new** — 6 tests covering both extRef paths, both error codes, version resolution, isFirstInstall semantics. |
| `lib/actions/install.ts` | shrunk from 58 → 43 lines. Session check + `recordInstall` + error translation. Return shape now `{ ok, installId, isFirstInstall, version }`. |
| `app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` | shrunk from 68 → 50 lines. Bearer auth + `recordInstall` + error translation. `version: "latest"` translated to `undefined` so the column never stores the sentinel string. |
| `components/extension/install-button.test.tsx` | mock shape updated. Button code unchanged — already only reads `result.ok`. |

## Behavior changes worth flagging

- **Every install now writes a row and bumps `downloadsCount`** (the (b) decision). The button's existing `isPending || installed` debounce + 3s reset is the (i) safeguard.
- **The `installs.version` column never receives `"latest"` again.** New web rows record the resolved semver. Existing legacy rows are still in the table — the SQL backfill from the design doc is optional.
- The atomic counter divergence (CLI did read-then-write) is fixed — both surfaces now go through the `sql\`+ 1\`` path inside the transaction.
- `downloadsCount` semantically redefines from "distinct users" to "total install events." For a small dataset this is cosmetic; if it matters, a separate query / column captures the prior definition.

## Not done

- A one-shot SQL to backfill legacy `version = "latest"` rows. Easy to skip.
- Updating `docs/architecture.md` to reflect the new install-recording module — worth doing alongside the commit since the doc currently describes the old duplicated path.

Want me to update the doc and commit, or stop here for review first?

> DEVELOPER
