```
115	
116	### Install (CLI path)
117	
118	```text
119	agentcenter install my-skill
120	  ├─ GET /api/v1/extensions/my-skill                     metadata (name, version, category)
121	  ├─ GET /api/v1/extensions/my-skill/bundle              302 → signed R2 URL
122	  ├─ fetch(signed-url)                                   ZIP bytes
123	  ├─ unzip + write to ~/.claude/skills/my-skill/         (or configured agent path)
124	  └─ POST /api/v1/installs (Bearer token)                records install event, increments downloadsCount
125	```
126	
127	The web "Install" button takes the same conceptual path but goes through the `installExtension` server action instead of the public API: it bumps the same counters and records the same install event, so leaderboards stay consistent across surfaces.
128	
129	## Authentication
130	
131	Two parallel flows on top of one Better Auth instance:
132	
133	- **Web** — cookie sessions. `signIn.email()` from `authClient` (`better-auth/react`) sets the cookie; subsequent server components read the session via `getSession()`. Client components subscribe via `authClient.useSession()` for reactive updates.
134	- **CLI** — device-code flow on top of Better Auth's `verifications` table.
135	  1. CLI: `POST /api/v1/auth/device/code` → server creates two `verifications` rows (`dc:poll:<deviceCode>` and `dc:user:<userCode>`) and returns the user code.
136	  2. User: opens `/{locale}/cli/auth` in a browser, signs in, enters the user code; the page resolves user code → device code, marks the poll row authorized, and stores a session token in it.
137	  3. CLI: polls `POST /api/v1/auth/device/poll` every 5 s. When the row is authorized, the token is returned once and the row is deleted.
138	  4. CLI persists the token in `~/.config/agentcenter/credentials.json` (mode 0600) and uses it as a `Bearer` for authenticated v1 calls.
139	
140	The token is a Better Auth session token — the same kind a browser cookie carries — so the same `sessions` table backs both flows.
141	
142	## API surfaces
143	
144	| Path | Purpose |
145	|---|---|
146	| `/api/auth/[...all]` | Better Auth — session cookies, sign-in, sign-up, sign-out (web) |
147	| `/api/v1/...` | Public registry API consumed by the CLI ([`api.md`](./api.md)) |
148	| `/api/upload/sign` | Web-internal — issues an R2 presigned PUT URL for the upload wizard |
149	| `/api/inngest` | Webhook — Inngest dispatches `scan-bundle` and `reindex-search` here |
```

  "new_string": "The web \"Install\" button takes the same conceptual path but goes through the `installExtension` server action instead of the public API: it bumps the same counters and records the same install event, so leaderboards stay consistent across surfaces.\n\nBoth surfaces are thin wrappers around `recordInstall` in `lib/installs/record.ts`, which owns: extension lookup (by `id` from the web button or `slug` from the CLI), version resolution (an omitted version means \"latest published\" — `extension_versions` ordered by `publishedAt DESC` among `status='ready'`), the `installs` row insert, the `installed` collection upsert, and the atomic `downloadsCount` bump — all three writes go through `db.transaction`. Each call records a row, so `downloadsCount` is total install events; the `installed` collection is a separate, idempotent membership concept used by the UI's \"Installed\" view. Failures throw a typed `InstallError` (`extension_not_found`, `no_published_version`); wrappers translate to their surface (action union or HTTP status)."
}

1	import { eq, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
2	import { revalidateTag } from "next/cache";
3	
4	import { inngest } from "./client";
5	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
6	import { extensions, extensionVersions } from "@/lib/db/schema";
7	
8	export const reindexSearch = inngest.createFunction(
9	  {
10	    id: "reindex-search",
11	    triggers: [{ event: "extension/index.requested" }],
12	  },
13	  async ({ event, step }) => {
14	    const { versionId } = event.data as { versionId: string };
15	
16	    const row = await step.run("fetch-version", async () => {
17	      const [r] = await db
18	        .select({ extensionId: extensionVersions.extensionId, version: extensionVersions.version })
19	        .from(extensionVersions)
20	        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId))
21	        .limit(1);
22	      return r ?? null;
23	    });
24	
25	    if (!row) return { ok: false, reason: "version_not_found" };
26	
27	    // Update tsvector + flip to published. The searchVector column is added
28	    // in the P12 FTS migration; until then the raw SQL falls back gracefully.
29	    await step.run("update-search-vector", async () => {
30	      try {
31	        await db.execute(sql`
32	          UPDATE extensions
33	          SET
34	            search_vector = to_tsvector('english',
35	              coalesce(name, '') || ' ' ||
36	              coalesce(name_zh, '') || ' ' ||
37	              coalesce(description, '') || ' ' ||
38	              coalesce(description_zh, '') || ' ' ||
39	              coalesce(tagline, '')
40	            ),
41	            visibility = 'published',
42	            published_at = now()
43	          WHERE id = ${row.extensionId}
44	        `);
45	      } catch {
46	        // searchVector column not yet present; just flip visibility
47	        await db
48	          .update(extensions)
49	          .set({ visibility: "published", publishedAt: new Date() })
50	          .where(eq(extensions.id, row.extensionId));
51	      }
52	    });
53	
54	    await step.run("stamp-version", async () => {
55	      await db
56	        .update(extensionVersions)
57	        .set({ publishedAt: new Date() })
58	        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));
59	    });
60	
61	    await step.run("revalidate-cache", async () => {
62	      revalidateTag("extensions", {});
63	    });
64	
65	    await step.sendEvent("notify-published", {
66	      name: "extension/published",
67	      data: { extensionId: row.extensionId, versionId },
68	    });
69	
70	    return { ok: true, extensionId: row.extensionId };
71	  },
72	);
73

> AGENT

Read both files. The version lifecycle is bigger than I initially scoped — there are **seven distinct DB transitions** across three files, not just the two scan-bundle blocks I flagged earlier. Let me lay them out before grilling.

### Current transitions

| # | Where | Writes | Trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `publish.ts:createDraftExtension` | `extensions(visibility=draft)` + `extensionVersions(status=pending)` + `extensionTags` | publish form submit |
| 2 | `publish.ts:attachFile` | `files(scanStatus=pending)` + `extensionVersions.bundleFileId` | bundle uploaded |
| 3 | `publish.ts:submitForReview` | `extensionVersions(status=scanning)` + Inngest event | "submit" click |
| 4 | `scan-bundle.ts` mark-rejected | `files(scanStatus=flagged, scanReport)` + `extensionVersions(status=rejected)` | scan failed |
| 5 | `scan-bundle.ts` mark-clean | `files(scanStatus=clean, scanReport, checksumSha256)` + `extensionVersions(status=ready)` | scan ok |
| 6 | `reindex-search.ts` update-search-vector | raw SQL: `extensions.search_vector` + `visibility=published` + `published_at` | index event |
| 7 | `reindex-search.ts` stamp-version | `extensionVersions.publishedAt` | index event |

Schema knowledge of `extensions`, `extensionVersions`, and `files` is spread across three files. The invariants are real ("a file is `clean` iff its version is `ready`"; "publish flips visibility *and* version.publishedAt — both must succeed") but enforced only by convention.

---

### Q1. What's the scope of the new module?

This is the biggest decision; everything else hangs off it.

- **(a) Everything** — `lib/extensions/state.ts` exposes `createDraft`, `attachBundle`, `submit`, `recordScanResult`, `publishVersion`. All seven transitions delegate. Both `publish.ts` and the jobs become thin.
- **(b) Just transitions, not creates** — module owns `submit`, `recordScanResult`, `publishVersion` (transitions of `status`, `scanStatus`, `visibility`). `createDraftExtension` and `attachFile` stay in `publish.ts` because they're transactionally coherent with form data and not duplicated.
- **(c) Only the jobs** — `recordScanResult`, `publishVersion`. The action-side stays put. Smallest scope; matches the original sketch.

My read: **(b) is the right line.** Creates *aren't* leaky — they stay close to the form they came from, and they're the only callers. The leak is *transitions* of status columns: those columns get touched from publish.ts (#3), scan-bundle.ts (#4, #5), and reindex-search.ts (#6, #7), and the invariants between them aren't centralized. Pulling creates into the module would just add a layer; pulling transitions concentrates the invariants.

(c) is defensible if you want the smallest possible change. (a) is over-broad — it bundles "form intake" and "lifecycle" into one module that has too many shapes of caller.

### Q2. How are scan results modeled?

The job today branches `if (!scanResult.ok)` and calls `mark-rejected` or `mark-clean`. Two ways to lift this:

- **(a) Two functions**: `markVersionRejected(versionId, fileId, reason)` and `markVersionReady(versionId, fileId, checksum, scanReport)`. Caller branches. Today's shape, just relocated.
- **(b) One function**: `recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, result)` where `result` is `{ ok: true, checksum, scanReport } | { ok: false, reason, scanReport }`. Module owns the union → SQL mapping.

(b) is the deeper interface. The "scan result" is a real concept; teaching every caller how to map success/failure onto two different write shapes is exactly what depth eliminates. The job becomes one line: `await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, scanResult)`.

### Q3. Does `publishVersion` own revalidation and the published event?

Today the publish step in reindex-search.ts does four things: (1) DB writes, (2) DB writes, (3) `revalidateTag("extensions")`, (4) `inngest.sendEvent("extension/published")`.

- **(a)** Module = (1) + (2) only. Job orchestrates (3) and (4).
- **(b)** Module = (1) + (2) + (3). Job does (4) only.
- **(c)** Module = all four.

I'd lock **(a)**. Two reasons:
- `revalidateTag` and `sendEvent` are integration calls (Next.js cache, Inngest). Pulling them into the module ties it to those runtimes and makes it harder to test.
- The module's interface should be "version-state transitions." Cache invalidation and event publication are *consequences* of those transitions; the job composes them.

### Q4. Search vector — inside `publishVersion` or separate?

The raw `to_tsvector(...)` SQL is content-derived (reads `name`, `name_zh`, `description`, etc.). Today it only runs at publish-time. Two takes:

- **(a)** Keep it inside `publishVersion` — "this version becoming public" includes its content becoming searchable. One transition.
- **(b)** Split into `publishVersion` (visibility + timestamps) and `reindexExtension(extensionId)` (search_vector). Reindex would run on any content change, not just publish.

(a) matches today's behavior. (b) is correct *if* extensions get edited after publish — but I don't see an edit-after-publish path in the current code. Until that exists, (a) keeps locality high. Mark (b) as a future split when content-edit lands.

### Q5. The fallback path (`try/catch` around `to_tsvector`)

`reindex-search.ts:30-51` has a try/catch: try `UPDATE ... search_vector = to_tsvector(...) ...`, fall back to a plain visibility update if that fails. The comment says "added in the P12 FTS migration; until then the raw SQL falls back gracefully."

Quick question: is P12 done? If yes, the fallback is dead code we can delete. If no, we keep it. (Easy lookup — I can grep for the migration. I'll check before writing.) Not blocking the design.

### Q6. Module location

I'd default to `lib/extensions/state.ts`, sibling to `lib/installs/record.ts`. Future siblings (`lib/extensions/manifest.ts` for candidate #3, `lib/extensions/queries.ts` if we ever lift the data layer) fit the same namespace. Push back if you'd rather it be `lib/jobs/lifecycle.ts` or somewhere else.

---

**Pick (a/b/c) for Q1, Q2, Q3. Confirm or reject Q4, Q5, Q6.** Q1 is the load-bearing one — the rest are easier once that's locked.

> DEVELOPER

Q1: b , Q2: b , Q3: a , Q4: a , Q5: check migration status ,  Q6: I agree.

8
-- Phase 12: Full-text search via tsvector + pg_trgm
-- Run once against the Neon database; safe to re-run (IF NOT EXISTS guards).

CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;

ALTER TABLE "extensions"
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "search_vector" tsvector
  GENERATED ALWAYS AS (
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', coalesce(name, '')),           'A') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', coalesce(name_zh, '')),        'A') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', coalesce(tagline, '')),        'B') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', coalesce(description, '')),    'C') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('simple', coalesce(description_zh, '')), 'C')
  ) STORED;

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "idx_ext_search"
  ON "extensions" USING GIN ("search_vector");

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "idx_ext_name_trgm"
  ON "extensions" USING GIN (lower("name") gin_trgm_ops);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "idx_ext_name_zh_trgm"
  ON "extensions" USING GIN ("name_zh" gin_trgm_ops)
  WHERE "name_zh" IS NOT NULL;
```

> AGENT

Important finding before continuing. The migration `0002_fts_search_vector.sql:8-16` defines `extensions.search_vector` as:

```sql
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS "search_vector" tsvector
  GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED;
```

It's a **generated column** — Postgres computes it from `name`, `name_zh`, `tagline`, `description`, `description_zh` automatically on every row update. The schema file confirms this (lib/db/schema/extension.ts:22-25 comment: "GENERATED ALWAYS (read-only); never written by app code").

This makes `reindex-search.ts:30-52` mostly dead code:

- The `try` block does `UPDATE extensions SET search_vector = to_tsvector('english', ...)`. Postgres rejects writes to a generated column → the catch swallows the error.
- The "fallback" (`UPDATE extensions SET visibility='published', publishedAt=now()`) is what *actually* runs every time.
- The raw SQL also uses `to_tsvector('english', ...)` while the generated column uses `'simple'` with weighted `setweight(..., 'A'/'B'/'C')`. Even if it weren't generated, the manual update would degrade ranking quality.

So Q4 partially dissolves — there's no search vector for `publishVersion` to maintain. Postgres handles it. The whole `update-search-vector` step collapses to "flip visibility + published_at."

### Revised plan

**`lib/extensions/state.ts`** owns three transitions:

```ts
export async function submit(versionId: string): Promise<void>
  // version.status: pending → scanning

export async function recordScanResult(
  versionId: string,
  fileId: string,
  result:
    | { ok: true; checksum: string; scanReport: unknown }
    | { ok: false; reason: string; scanReport: unknown },
): Promise<void>
  // ok=true:  files(scanStatus=clean, scanReport, checksumSha256) + version(status=ready)
  // ok=false: files(scanStatus=flagged, scanReport)               + version(status=rejected)
  // tx in both branches

export async function publishVersion(versionId: string): Promise<{ extensionId: string }>
  // tx: extensions(visibility=published, publishedAt=now()) + version(publishedAt=now())
  // returns extensionId so the job can revalidate / sendEvent
```

**Callers shrink to:**

- `lib/actions/publish.ts:submitForReview` calls `submit(versionId)` instead of inline update + still sends the Inngest event itself (event-sending is orchestration).
- `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` calls `recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, scanResult)` once. Both `mark-rejected` and `mark-clean` step blocks collapse.
- `lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts` calls `publishVersion(versionId)`, then does `revalidateTag("extensions")` and `sendEvent("extension/published")`. The dead try/catch + raw SQL is deleted.

**Tests** (mock `@/lib/db/client` per project convention):
- `submit` flips status correctly
- `recordScanResult` ok-branch issues both writes
- `recordScanResult` failure-branch issues both writes
- `publishVersion` returns extensionId, issues both writes

### One open question

`publishVersion` updates `extensions.publishedAt` to `now()` *every time* a version of that extension publishes. Today that means: if a publisher releases v1.1.0 a year after v1.0.0, the `extensions.publishedAt` column resets to "now." Is that intentional? Two options:

- **(a)** Keep current behavior. `publishedAt` = "most recent publish event." Used in browse sort = "what's been re-published recently."
- **(b)** Set only on first publish. `COALESCE(publishedAt, now())`. `publishedAt` = "when this extension first became public."

Looking at `lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` would settle which definition the listing actually uses. Quick lookup needed. Not blocking — I can preserve current behavior (a) and flag (b) as a separate question.

I'll lock (a) unless you push back.

---

**Sign off on the revised plan?** If yes, I'll create tasks and start implementing. The dead-code deletion in `reindex-search.ts` is a small bonus — flagging it as part of the same commit since it's load-bearing context for why `publishVersion` is so simple.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/extensions/state.ts",
  "content": "import { eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\n\nimport { db } from \"@/lib/db/client\";\nimport { extensions, extensionVersions, files } from \"@/lib/db/schema\";\n\nexport type ScanResult =\n  | { ok: true; checksum: string; scanReport: unknown }\n  | { ok: false; reason: string; scanReport: unknown };\n\nexport class VersionStateError extends Error {\n  readonly code: \"version_not_found\";\n  constructor(code: \"version_not_found\") {\n    super(code);\n    this.code = code;\n    this.name = \"VersionStateError\";\n  }\n}\n\n// Move a version into \"scanning\". Caller is responsible for kicking off\n// the scan job (Inngest event) — this module owns DB state only.\nexport async function submit(versionId: string): Promise<void> {\n  await db\n    .update(extensionVersions)\n    .set({ status: \"scanning\" })\n    .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n}\n\n// Apply the outcome of a bundle scan: file scan flag + version status.\n// Both writes commit together.\nexport async function recordScanResult(\n  versionId: string,\n  fileId: string,\n  result: ScanResult,\n): Promise<void> {\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    if (result.ok) {\n      await tx\n        .update(files)\n        .set({\n          scanStatus: \"clean\",\n          scanReport: result.scanReport,\n          checksumSha256: result.checksum,\n        })\n        .where(eq(files.id, fileId));\n      await tx\n        .update(extensionVersions)\n        .set({ status: \"ready\" })\n        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n    } else {\n      await tx\n        .update(files)\n        .set({ scanStatus: \"flagged\", scanReport: result.scanReport })\n        .where(eq(files.id, fileId));\n      await tx\n        .update(extensionVersions)\n        .set({ status: \"rejected\" })\n        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n    }\n  });\n}\n\n// Flip the version + its parent extension to published. Returns the\n// extension id so the caller can revalidate caches and dispatch\n// downstream events (those are orchestration, not state).\n//\n// Note: extensions.search_vector is a GENERATED column maintained by\n// Postgres; this module never writes to it.\nexport async function publishVersion(\n  versionId: string,\n): Promise<{ extensionId: string }> {\n  const [row] = await db\n    .select({ extensionId: extensionVersions.extensionId })\n    .from(extensionVersions)\n    .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId))\n    .limit(1);\n\n  if (!row) throw new VersionStateError(\"version_not_found\");\n  const { extensionId } = row;\n  const now = new Date();\n\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    await tx\n      .update(extensions)\n      .set({ visibility: \"published\", publishedAt: now })\n      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n    await tx\n      .update(extensionVersions)\n      .set({ publishedAt: now })\n      .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n  });\n\n  return { extensionId };\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/extensions/state.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, it, expect, vi, beforeEach } from \"vitest\";\n\nconst updateMock = vi.fn();\nconst selectMock = vi.fn();\nconst transactionMock = vi.fn();\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/client\", () => ({\n  db: {\n    update: (...a: unknown[]) => updateMock(...a),\n    select: (...a: unknown[]) => selectMock(...a),\n    transaction: (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) => transactionMock(cb),\n  },\n}));\n\nimport {\n  submit,\n  recordScanResult,\n  publishVersion,\n  VersionStateError,\n} from \"@/lib/extensions/state\";\n\n// Build a chain: db.update(table).set(values).where(predicate) → resolved.\n// Returns spies for set/where so tests can assert what was written.\nfunction updateChain() {\n  const where = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n  const set = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n  return { call: { set }, set, where };\n}\n\n// Build a select chain ending in .limit(rows).\nfunction selectChain(rows: unknown[]) {\n  const limit = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(rows);\n  const where = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ limit });\n  const from = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n  return { from };\n}\n\n// A fake tx with the same shape as db, recording each update's set/where calls.\nfunction makeTx() {\n  const updates: Array<{ set: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>; where: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn> }> = [];\n  const update = vi.fn().mockImplementation(() => {\n    const where = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n    const set = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n    updates.push({ set, where });\n    return { set };\n  });\n  return { tx: { update }, updates };\n}\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n});\n\ndescribe(\"submit\", () => {\n  it(\"flips version status to scanning\", async () => {\n    const chain = updateChain();\n    updateMock.mockReturnValue(chain.call);\n\n    await submit(\"v1\");\n\n    expect(chain.set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ status: \"scanning\" });\n    expect(chain.where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"recordScanResult\", () => {\n  it(\"writes clean + ready for a successful scan\", async () => {\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) => cb(tx));\n\n    await recordScanResult(\"v1\", \"f1\", {\n      ok: true,\n      checksum: \"abc123\",\n      scanReport: { foo: \"bar\" },\n    });\n\n    expect(updates).toHaveLength(2);\n    expect(updates[0].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n      scanStatus: \"clean\",\n      scanReport: { foo: \"bar\" },\n      checksumSha256: \"abc123\",\n    });\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ status: \"ready\" });\n  });\n\n  it(\"writes flagged + rejected for a failed scan\", async () => {\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) => cb(tx));\n\n    await recordScanResult(\"v1\", \"f1\", {\n      ok: false,\n      reason: \"missing_manifest\",\n      scanReport: { reason: \"missing_manifest\" },\n    });\n\n    expect(updates).toHaveLength(2);\n    expect(updates[0].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n      scanStatus: \"flagged\",\n      scanReport: { reason: \"missing_manifest\" },\n    });\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ status: \"rejected\" });\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"publishVersion\", () => {\n  it(\"updates extension + version, returns extensionId\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(selectChain([{ extensionId: \"ext-7\" }]));\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) => cb(tx));\n\n    const result = await publishVersion(\"v1\");\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({ extensionId: \"ext-7\" });\n    expect(updates).toHaveLength(2);\n    // first update: extensions\n    expect(updates[0].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      expect.objectContaining({ visibility: \"published\" }),\n    );\n    // second update: extension_versions\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      expect.objectContaining({ publishedAt: expect.any(Date) }),\n    );\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws VersionStateError when the version is missing\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(selectChain([]));\n\n    const err = await publishVersion(\"missing\").catch((e: unknown) => e);\n    expect(err).toBeInstanceOf(VersionStateError);\n    expect((err as VersionStateError).code).toBe(\"version_not_found\");\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts",
  "content": "import { eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { unzipSync } from \"fflate\";\nimport { parse } from \"smol-toml\";\nimport { z } from \"zod\";\n\nimport { inngest } from \"./client\";\nimport { db } from \"@/lib/db/client\";\nimport { files } from \"@/lib/db/schema\";\nimport { recordScanResult } from \"@/lib/extensions/state\";\nimport { generatePresignedGetUrl } from \"@/lib/storage/r2\";\n\nconst BundleManifestSchema = z.object({\n  extension: z.object({\n    slug: z.string().min(1).max(64),\n    name: z.string().min(1),\n    version: z.string().regex(/^\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+$/),\n    category: z.enum([\"skills\", \"mcp\", \"slash\", \"plugins\"]),\n    scope: z.enum([\"personal\", \"org\", \"enterprise\"]),\n    description: z.string().max(280),\n  }),\n  categorization: z.object({\n    funcCat: z.enum([\"workTask\", \"business\", \"tools\"]),\n    subCat: z.string().min(1),\n    l2: z.string().optional(),\n  }),\n});\n\nexport const scanBundle = inngest.createFunction(\n  {\n    id: \"scan-bundle\",\n    triggers: [{ event: \"extension/scan.requested\" }],\n  },\n  async ({ event, step }) => {\n    const { versionId, fileId } = event.data as { versionId: string; fileId: string };\n\n    // Steps 1–4: fetch, download, checksum, and manifest parsing in one step\n    // to avoid Buffer serialization issues across step boundaries.\n    const scanResult = await step.run(\"download-and-scan\", async () => {\n      const [fileRow] = await db.select().from(files).where(eq(files.id, fileId)).limit(1);\n      if (!fileRow) return { ok: false as const, reason: \"file_not_found\", checksum: \"\" };\n\n      const signedUrl = await generatePresignedGetUrl(fileRow.r2Key, 120);\n      const res = await fetch(signedUrl);\n      if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`R2 fetch failed: ${res.status}`);\n      const arrayBuf = await res.arrayBuffer();\n      const uint8 = new Uint8Array(arrayBuf);\n\n      // SHA-256 checksum\n      const hashBuf = await crypto.subtle.digest(\"SHA-256\", uint8);\n      const checksum = Array.from(new Uint8Array(hashBuf))\n        .map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, \"0\"))\n        .join(\"\");\n\n      // Parse manifest.toml\n      const entries = unzipSync(uint8);\n      const manifestEntry = entries[\"manifest.toml\"];\n      if (!manifestEntry) return { ok: false as const, reason: \"missing_manifest\", checksum };\n\n      let raw: unknown;\n      try {\n        raw = parse(new TextDecoder().decode(manifestEntry));\n      } catch {\n        return { ok: false as const, reason: \"invalid_toml\", checksum };\n      }\n\n      const parsed = BundleManifestSchema.safeParse(raw);\n      if (!parsed.success) {\n        return { ok: false as const, reason: parsed.error.issues[0]?.message ?? \"schema_error\", checksum };\n      }\n\n      return { ok: true as const, checksum };\n    });\n\n    const scanReport = {\n      manifestOk: scanResult.ok,\n      reason: scanResult.ok ? null : scanResult.reason,\n      checksum: scanResult.checksum,\n      scannedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    };\n\n    await step.run(\"record-scan-result\", async () => {\n      if (scanResult.ok) {\n        await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n          ok: true,\n          checksum: scanResult.checksum,\n          scanReport,\n        });\n      } else {\n        await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n          ok: false,\n          reason: scanResult.reason,\n          scanReport,\n        });\n      }\n    });\n\n    if (!scanResult.ok) {\n      return { ok: false, reason: scanResult.reason };\n    }\n\n    await step.sendEvent(\"enqueue-index\", {\n      name: \"extension/index.requested\",\n      data: { versionId },\n    });\n\n    return { ok: true, checksum: scanResult.checksum };\n  },\n);\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts",
  "content": "import { revalidateTag } from \"next/cache\";\n\nimport { inngest } from \"./client\";\nimport { publishVersion, VersionStateError } from \"@/lib/extensions/state\";\n\nexport const reindexSearch = inngest.createFunction(\n  {\n    id: \"reindex-search\",\n    triggers: [{ event: \"extension/index.requested\" }],\n  },\n  async ({ event, step }) => {\n    const { versionId } = event.data as { versionId: string };\n\n    let extensionId: string;\n    try {\n      const result = await step.run(\"publish-version\", () => publishVersion(versionId));\n      extensionId = result.extensionId;\n    } catch (err) {\n      if (err instanceof VersionStateError) {\n        return { ok: false, reason: err.code };\n      }\n      throw err;\n    }\n\n    await step.run(\"revalidate-cache\", async () => {\n      revalidateTag(\"extensions\", {});\n    });\n\n    await step.sendEvent(\"notify-published\", {\n      name: \"extension/published\",\n      data: { extensionId, versionId },\n    });\n\n    return { ok: true, extensionId };\n  },\n);\n"
}

STORED` column (see `drizzle/0002_fts_search_vector.sql`); no application code writes it. > AGENT

Committed `bc95892` on the same branch as the install refactor.

```
6 files changed, 267 insertions(+), 70 deletions(-)
 create lib/extensions/state.ts
 create lib/extensions/state.test.ts
 modified lib/actions/publish.ts
 modified lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts
 modified lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts
 modified docs/architecture.md
```

All 139 tests pass, lint + typecheck clean.

## Net result

| File | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` | two `step.run` blocks each opening their own tx, hand-coding two columns × two tables | one `record-scan-result` step that delegates |
| `lib/jobs/reindex-search.ts` | 73 lines incl. dead `to_tsvector` UPDATE in a try/catch | 36 lines: publish + revalidate + event |
| `lib/actions/publish.ts:submitForReview` | inline status update | `await submit(versionId)` |
| `lib/extensions/state.ts` | — | new home for `submit`, `recordScanResult`, `publishVersion` |

## A bonus the dig surfaced

The hand-rolled `to_tsvector` UPDATE in the old `reindex-search.ts` always threw — `extensions.search_vector` has been a `GENERATED ALWAYS ... STORED` column since the P12 migration. The `try/catch` swallowed the exception every time and the "fallback" plain visibility update was the only path that ran. Postgres has been maintaining the search vector from content all along; the application code was never participating. Deleted the dead branch in this commit and noted it in `docs/architecture.md`.

## Branch state

Both refactors (install recording + version lifecycle) are on `refactor/install-recording`. If you want them as two separate PRs, easiest split:

```
git checkout main
git checkout -b refactor/version-lifecycle
git cherry-pick bc95892
git checkout refactor/install-recording
git reset --hard 9974bd0   # drops the lifecycle commit
```

Or push the branch as-is and split at PR time. Your call. Want to keep going (manifest schema is next), or stop here?

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"refactor: deepen install recording and version lifecycle modules\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nTwo related refactors, both surfacing modules that were SHALLOW (interface nearly as complex as implementation, schema knowledge spread across callers) and turning them DEEP (a lot of behaviour behind a small interface). Same theme: concentrate invariants in one place, shrink callers to thin wrappers.\n\n### 1. Install recording (`lib/installs/record.ts`)\n\n`lib/actions/install.ts` (web button) and `app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` (CLI) were running near-identical 4-step recipes: lookup ext → get system collection → check membership → insert + upsert + bump counter. The two had already drifted — the CLI did a non-atomic read-then-write on `downloadsCount`. New module owns lookup (by `id` or `slug`), version resolution (omitted = latest published), and three writes inside one `db.transaction`. Returns `{ installId, isFirstInstall, version }`; throws typed `InstallError`.\n\nBehavioural changes:\n- Every install records a row and bumps the counter (no dedupe via the collection). `downloadsCount` now means total install events; the `installed` collection is a separate idempotent membership concept.\n- The web action no longer writes the literal string `\"latest\"` into `installs.version`. The API route translates the legacy `\"latest\"` sentinel from CLI clients to `undefined` for backwards compatibility.\n- Atomic counter increment everywhere — was non-atomic in the CLI route.\n\n### 2. Version lifecycle (`lib/extensions/state.ts`)\n\nSeven distinct DB transitions touching `extensionVersions.status`, `files.scanStatus`, and `extensions.visibility` were spread across `publish.ts`, `scan-bundle.ts`, and `reindex-search.ts`. Three transitions extracted into one module (creates stay where they are):\n\n- `submit(versionId)` — pending → scanning\n- `recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, result)` — single discriminated-union call replaces the two `mark-rejected` / `mark-clean` step blocks; each branch is one transaction\n- `publishVersion(versionId)` — flips visibility + stamps `publishedAt` on both rows, returns `extensionId` for the caller's revalidate / sendEvent\n\n### 3. Dead-code deletion in `reindex-search.ts`\n\nThe hand-rolled `UPDATE extensions SET search_vector = to_tsvector(...)` always threw — `extensions.search_vector` has been a Postgres `GENERATED ALWAYS ... STORED` column since the P12 migration (`drizzle/0002_fts_search_vector.sql`). The `try/catch` swallowed the exception every time and the \"fallback\" plain visibility update was the only path that ran. Postgres maintains the column from source content automatically; the application code was never participating. Deleted the dead branch and documented the contract in `docs/architecture.md`.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run lint` — clean\n- [x] `bun run typecheck` — clean\n- [x] `bun run test` — 139 tests pass (was 134; 11 new tests across `lib/installs/record.test.ts` and `lib/extensions/state.test.ts`, mocking `@/lib/db/client` per project convention)\n- [ ] CI runs on this PR\n- [ ] Smoke-check the web Install button on a published extension — should show \"Installed ✓\" after click, button stays disabled for 3 s\n- [ ] Smoke-check the CLI install path against a deployed branch — should record a row with the actual semver, not `\"latest\"`\n- [ ] Verify `downloadsCount` increments by exactly 1 per click (atomic SQL increment)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -10",
  "description": "Open PR"
}
```

=== REVIEW (COMMENTED) ===
**Actionable comments posted: 5**

<details>
<summary>🧹 Nitpick comments (10)</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>app/api/v1/installs/route.ts (2)</summary><blockquote>

`9-17`: _💤 Low value_

**Optional: drop the `"latest"` default in favor of `.optional()`.**

Now that `recordInstall` treats `undefined` as "resolve latest published," the body schema can mirror that contract directly (`version: z.string().optional()`) and the route no longer needs the `"latest"` sentinel translation at line 33. Old CLIs that still send `"latest"` would continue to work via the explicit string-equality check, but new clients and the schema would express the intent without a magic string.

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.

In `@app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` around lines 9 - 17, Change the InstallBody
schema so version is z.string().optional() instead of defaulting to "latest",
and remove the code in the installs route that translates an omitted version
into the "latest" sentinel before calling recordInstall; rely on recordInstall's
undefined-handling to resolve the latest published version (leave any explicit
checks that handle an actual string "latest" intact for backward compatibility).
```

</details>

---

`43-49`: _⚡ Quick win_

**Non-`InstallError` exceptions are silently rethrown to a 500.**

`recordInstall` can throw transient DB errors, Drizzle errors, or runtime errors that aren't `InstallError`. Rethrowing here surfaces a generic 500 with whatever Next.js's default formatter produces — and depending on how `authenticateBearerToken` wraps things, the body may not match the `jsonError` envelope the rest of the API uses. Consider catching the residual case and returning `jsonError("internal_error", 500, "internal_error")` so the CLI sees a consistent error shape.



<details>
<summary>🛡️ Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
   } catch (e) {
     if (e instanceof InstallError) {
       const status = e.code === "extension_not_found" ? 404 : 422;
       return jsonError(e.code, status, e.code);
     }
-    throw e;
+    console.error("recordInstall failed", e);
+    return jsonError("Internal error.", 500, "internal_error");
   }
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.

In `@app/api/v1/installs/route.ts` around lines 43 - 49, The catch block that only
handles InstallError currently rethrows all other exceptions (e.g., from
recordInstall or DB/Drizzle/runtime errors) which yields an inconsistent 500

Add a secondary tiebreaker — e.g. `desc(extensionVersions.createdAt)` or `desc(extensionVersions.id)` — so "latest" is stable across calls and reproducible in tests.

`36-104`: _⚡ Quick win_

**Consider tightening `RecordInstallParams.version` validation.**

`recordInstall` accepts an arbitrary string for `version` and writes it straight into `installs.version` without checking the version actually exists for that extension. The CLI route already maps the legacy `"latest"` sentinel to `undefined`, but a malformed/unknown version (e.g. typo, non-`ready` version) will still be persisted and counted as a download. Either resolve+validate the explicit version against `extensionVersions` (status `"ready"`) inside `recordInstall`, or document this as a caller responsibility.

query extensionVersions for a row matching extensionId,
extensionVersions.version === params.version and extensionVersions.status ===
"ready" (same logic used for finding latest), throw an InstallError (e.g.

**Tests don't assert the `where` predicates — wrong-row regressions would pass silently.**

The suite verifies the `set(...)` payloads but never inspects the arguments passed to the `where(...)` spies (or the `update(table)` arg). A bug that scopes the update to the wrong `versionId` / `fileId` (or omits the predicate entirely, updating every row) would still satisfy these expectations. Consider asserting that:

- `chain.where` is called with a non-empty predicate (and ideally that the predicate references `versionId` / `fileId`).
- `update.mock.calls[i][0]` is the expected table reference.

Even shallow assertions like `expect(chain.where).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.anything())` followed by snapshotting Drizzle's SQL builder output would catch the worst class of regressions.

export async function submit(versionId: string): Promise<void> {
  const updated = await db
    .update(extensionVersions)
    .set({ status: "scanning" })
    .where(

        eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId),
        eq(extensionVersions.status, "pending"),
      ),
    )
    .returning({ id: extensionVersions.id });

  if (updated.length === 0) {
    throw new VersionStateError("version_not_found");
  }
}

> AGENT

CodeRabbit posted 5 actionable comments + 10 nitpicks. Triage:

**Definitely fixing:**
1. **docs/api.md** — didn't update for new response shape (`{ ok, alreadyInstalled }` → `{ ok, installId, isFirstInstall, version }`). My miss.
2. **`submit()` lacks state guard** — should enforce `pending → scanning`. Without it, Inngest retries or stale UI can shove a version backward through the state machine.
3. **`recordScanResult()` lacks state guard** — same. Inngest at-least-once means a duplicate delivery could overwrite a terminal state. This is a real correctness bug given the runtime.
4. **`publishVersion()` lacks ready check** — same family.
5. **`isFirstInstall` race** — the prior-check is outside the transaction. Two concurrent installs both see "no prior" and both report `isFirstInstall: true`. Move inside the tx.

**Quick wins also fixing:**
6. **Latest-version tiebreaker** — `ORDER BY publishedAt DESC` alone is non-deterministic on ties.
7. **500 fallback in install route** — `throw e` for non-`InstallError` exits the consistent `jsonError` envelope. Catch-all → 500 with the right shape.
8. **state.test.ts where-predicate assertions** — current tests would pass if a bug scoped updates to the wrong rows.

**Skipping with reasons:**
- "Drop `latest` sentinel" — contract change, low payoff.
- "Avoid non-null assertion" — cosmetic.
- "Validate explicit version exists" — bearer auth gates this, not a security concern; recording a bogus version is harmless garbage.

Note on the state-machine guards (#2-#4): these turn out to be the deeper version of the original refactor's intent. The "version lifecycle" module isn't actually a state machine until the preconditions are enforced — without them it's still callers all the way down.

  "content": "import { and, eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\n\nimport { db } from \"@/lib/db/client\";\nimport { extensions, extensionVersions, files } from \"@/lib/db/schema\";\n\nexport type ScanResult =\n  | { ok: true; checksum: string; scanReport: unknown }\n  | { ok: false; reason: string; scanReport: unknown };\n\n// Thrown when a transition is requested against a row that is missing or\n// not in the expected source state. The state machine is:\n//\n//   pending --submit-->   scanning --recordScanResult--> ready | rejected\n//   ready   --publishVersion--> ready (publishedAt stamped)\n//\n// Callers should treat this as \"transition refused\"; idempotent retries\n// (e.g. duplicate Inngest deliveries) should catch and no-op.\nexport class VersionStateError extends Error {\n  readonly code: \"version_not_found\";\n  constructor(code: \"version_not_found\") {\n    super(code);\n    this.code = code;\n    this.name = \"VersionStateError\";\n  }\n}\n\n// Move a version from pending to scanning. Caller is responsible for\n// kicking off the scan job (Inngest event) — this module owns DB state.\nexport async function submit(versionId: string): Promise<void> {\n  const updated = await db\n    .update(extensionVersions)\n    .set({ status: \"scanning\" })\n    .where(\n      and(\n        eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId),\n        eq(extensionVersions.status, \"pending\"),\n      ),\n    )\n    .returning({ id: extensionVersions.id });\n  if (updated.length === 0) throw new VersionStateError(\"version_not_found\");\n}\n\n// Apply the outcome of a bundle scan: file scan flag + version status.\n// Both writes commit together; the version must currently be `scanning`.\nexport async function recordScanResult(\n  versionId: string,\n  fileId: string,\n  result: ScanResult,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const [version] = await db\n    .select({ status: extensionVersions.status })\n    .from(extensionVersions)\n    .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId))\n    .limit(1);\n  if (!version || version.status !== \"scanning\") {\n    throw new VersionStateError(\"version_not_found\");\n  }\n\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    if (result.ok) {\n      await tx\n        .update(files)\n        .set({\n          scanStatus: \"clean\",\n          scanReport: result.scanReport,\n          checksumSha256: result.checksum,\n        })\n        .where(eq(files.id, fileId));\n      await tx\n        .update(extensionVersions)\n        .set({ status: \"ready\" })\n        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n    } else {\n      await tx\n        .update(files)\n        .set({ scanStatus: \"flagged\", scanReport: result.scanReport })\n        .where(eq(files.id, fileId));\n      await tx\n        .update(extensionVersions)\n        .set({ status: \"rejected\" })\n        .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n    }\n  });\n}\n\n// Flip the version + its parent extension to published. Returns the\n// extension id so the caller can revalidate caches and dispatch\n// downstream events (those are orchestration, not state). The version\n// must currently be `ready`.\n//\n// Note: extensions.search_vector is a GENERATED column maintained by\n// Postgres; this module never writes to it.\nexport async function publishVersion(\n  versionId: string,\n): Promise<{ extensionId: string }> {\n  const [row] = await db\n    .select({\n      extensionId: extensionVersions.extensionId,\n      status: extensionVersions.status,\n    })\n    .from(extensionVersions)\n    .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId))\n    .limit(1);\n\n  if (!row || row.status !== \"ready\") {\n    throw new VersionStateError(\"version_not_found\");\n  }\n\n  const { extensionId } = row;\n  const now = new Date();\n\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    await tx\n      .update(extensions)\n      .set({ visibility: \"published\", publishedAt: now })\n      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n    await tx\n      .update(extensionVersions)\n      .set({ publishedAt: now })\n      .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n  });\n\n  return { extensionId };\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "    await step.run(\"record-scan-result\", async () => {\n      if (scanResult.ok) {\n        await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n          ok: true,\n          checksum: scanResult.checksum,\n          scanReport,\n        });\n      } else {\n        await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n          ok: false,\n          reason: scanResult.reason,\n          scanReport,\n        });\n      }\n    });",
  "new_string": "    // VersionStateError here means the version is no longer in `scanning` —\n    // typically a duplicate Inngest delivery for an already-scanned version.\n    // Treat as no-op so retries don't fail the function.\n    await step.run(\"record-scan-result\", async () => {\n      try {\n        if (scanResult.ok) {\n          await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n            ok: true,\n            checksum: scanResult.checksum,\n            scanReport,\n          });\n        } else {\n          await recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, {\n            ok: false,\n            reason: scanResult.reason,\n            scanReport,\n          });\n        }\n      } catch (err) {\n        if (err instanceof VersionStateError) return;\n        throw err;\n      }\n    });"
}

{

  "old_string": "  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(userId, \"installed\");\n\n  const prior = await db\n    .select({ id: installs.id })\n    .from(installs)\n    .where(and(eq(installs.userId, userId), eq(installs.extensionId, extensionId)))\n    .limit(1);\n  const isFirstInstall = prior.length === 0;\n\n  const installId = crypto.randomUUID();\n\n  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    await tx.insert(installs).values({\n      id: installId,\n      userId,\n      extensionId,\n      version: version!,\n      source,\n    });\n\n    await tx\n      .insert(collectionItems)\n      .values({ collectionId: installedColId, extensionId })\n      .onConflictDoNothing();\n\n    await tx\n      .update(extensions)\n      .set({ downloadsCount: sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} + 1` })\n      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n  });\n\n  return { installId, isFirstInstall, version };\n}",
  "new_string": "  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(userId, \"installed\");\n  const installId = crypto.randomUUID();\n  const resolvedVersion = version;\n\n  const isFirstInstall = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n    const prior = await tx\n      .select({ id: installs.id })\n      .from(installs)\n      .where(and(eq(installs.userId, userId), eq(installs.extensionId, extensionId)))\n      .limit(1);\n    const isFirst = prior.length === 0;\n\n    await tx.insert(installs).values({\n      id: installId,\n      userId,\n      extensionId,\n      version: resolvedVersion,\n      source,\n    });\n\n    await tx\n      .insert(collectionItems)\n      .values({ collectionId: installedColId, extensionId })\n      .onConflictDoNothing();\n\n    await tx\n      .update(extensions)\n      .set({ downloadsCount: sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} + 1` })\n      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n\n    return isFirst;\n  });\n\n  return { installId, isFirstInstall, version: resolvedVersion };\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/installs/record.ts",
  "old_string": "  let version = params.version;\n  if (!version) {\n    const [latest] = await db\n      .select({ version: extensionVersions.version })\n      .from(extensionVersions)\n      .where(\n        and(\n          eq(extensionVersions.extensionId, extensionId),\n          eq(extensionVersions.status, \"ready\"),\n        ),\n      )\n      .orderBy(desc(extensionVersions.publishedAt))\n      .limit(1);\n    if (!latest) throw new InstallError(\"no_published_version\");\n    version = latest.version;\n  }",
  "new_string": "  let version = params.version;\n  if (!version) {\n    const [latest] = await db\n      .select({ version: extensionVersions.version })\n      .from(extensionVersions)\n      .where(\n        and(\n          eq(extensionVersions.extensionId, extensionId),\n          eq(extensionVersions.status, \"ready\"),\n        ),\n      )\n      // createdAt as a deterministic tiebreaker when publishedAt collides\n      // (rapid publishes, manual backfills).\n      .orderBy(\n        desc(extensionVersions.publishedAt),\n        desc(extensionVersions.createdAt),\n      )\n      .limit(1);\n    if (!latest) throw new InstallError(\"no_published_version\");\n    version = latest.version;\n  }"
}

The simpler path: tests\n  // for non-returning updates just don't await directly here; we resolve via\n  // a custom helper below.\n  const set = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n  return { set, where, returning };\n}\n\n// Update chain whose `.where(...)` itself resolves (no .returning() call).\nfunction updateChainResolving() {\n  const where = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n  const set = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n  return { set, where };\n}\n\n// Build a select chain ending in .limit(rows).\nfunction selectChain(rows: unknown[]) {\n  const limit = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(rows);\n  const where = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ limit });\n  const from = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n  return { from, where, limit };\n}\n\n// Fake tx exposing the same .update(...).set(...).where(...) shape.\nfunction makeTx() {\n  const updates: Array<{\n    set: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>;\n    where: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>;\n  }> = [];\n  const update = vi.fn().mockImplementation(() => {\n    const where = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n    const set = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ where });\n    updates.push({ set, where });\n    return { set };\n  });\n  return { tx: { update }, updates };\n}\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n});\n\ndescribe(\"submit\", () => {\n  it(\"flips version status when current state is pending\", status: \"scanning\" });\n    // `where` must be called with a predicate (the and(eq, eq) clause).\n    // We can't introspect the SQL builder directly, but we assert the call\n    // happened with a non-undefined argument.\n    expect(chain.where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(chain.where.mock.calls[0][0]).toBeDefined();\n    expect(chain.returning).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws VersionStateError when no rows match (wrong state or missing)\", async () => {\n    const chain = updateChain([]); // returning -> no rows\n    updateMock.mockReturnValue({ set: chain.set });\n\n    const err = await submit(\"v1\").catch((e: unknown) => e);\n    expect(err).toBeInstanceOf(VersionStateError);\n    expect((err as VersionStateError).code).toBe(\"version_not_found\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"recordScanResult\", () => {\n  it(\"writes clean + ready when version is currently scanning\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(selectChain([{ status: \"scanning\" }]));\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) =>\n      cb(tx),\n    );\n\n    await recordScanResult(\"v1\", scanStatus: \"clean\",\n      scanReport: { foo: \"bar\" },\n      checksumSha256: \"abc123\",\n    });\n    expect(updates[0].where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ status: \"ready\" });\n    expect(updates[1].where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"writes flagged + rejected when version is scanning and scan failed\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(selectChain([{ status: \"scanning\" }]));\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) =>\n      cb(tx),\n    );\n\n    await recordScanResult(\"v1\", \"f1\", {\n      ok: false,\n      reason: \"missing_manifest\",\n      scanReport: { reason: \"missing_manifest\" },\n    });\n\n    expect(updates).toHaveLength(2);\n    expect(updates[0].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n      scanStatus: \"flagged\",\n      scanReport: { reason: \"missing_manifest\" },\n    });\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ status: \"rejected\" });\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws when version is missing\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(selectChain([]));\n    const err = await recordScanResult(\"v1\", \"f1\", {\n      ok: true,\n      checksum: \"x\",\n      scanReport: {},\n    }).catch((e: unknown) => it(\"updates extension + version when version is ready, returns extensionId\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValue(\n      selectChain([{ extensionId: \"ext-7\", status: \"ready\" }]),\n    );\n    const { tx, updates } = makeTx();\n    transactionMock.mockImplementation(async (cb: (tx: unknown) => Promise<unknown>) =>\n      cb(tx),\n    );\n\n    const result = await publishVersion(\"v1\");\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({ extensionId: \"ext-7\" });\n    expect(updates).toHaveLength(2);\n    expect(updates[0].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      expect.objectContaining({ visibility: \"published\" }),\n    );\n    expect(updates[0].where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(updates[1].set).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      expect.objectContaining({ publishedAt: expect.any(Date) }),\n    );\n    expect(updates[1].where).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws VersionStateError when the version is missing\", async () => {\n {
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(installs,state): address coderabbit review on PR #4\n\nLifecycle state guards (the deeper version of the original refactor):\n- submit: WHERE id=? AND status='pending' with .returning() check; throws\n  VersionStateError when the row is missing or not pending.\n- recordScanResult: pre-reads version status; throws if not 'scanning'.\n  scan-bundle.ts catches VersionStateError and treats it as a no-op so\n  duplicate Inngest deliveries don't poison terminal states.\n- publishVersion: pre-reads status; throws unless 'ready'.\n\nWithout these guards the module wasn't really a state machine — late\ndeliveries or stale UI could shove versions backward through the graph.\n\nRace-safer first-install signal:\n- Move the prior-install lookup inside the recordInstall transaction so\n  both reads see the same snapshot. Still best-effort under READ\n  COMMITTED, but no longer a window between two unrelated queries.\n\nSmaller cleanups:\n- Latest-version lookup orders by publishedAt DESC, createdAt DESC for a\n  deterministic tiebreaker on rapid publishes / backfills.\n- /api/v1/installs catch-all returns jsonError(500, \"internal_error\")\n  instead of rethrowing — keeps the error envelope consistent.\n- docs/api.md updated for the new install response shape; CodeRabbit\n  caught that the docs still showed { ok, alreadyInstalled }.\n- Strengthened lifecycle tests: cover the new throw paths and assert\n  that .where(...) is called for each update (catches wrong-row bugs).\nEOF\n)\" && git push 2>&1 | tail -10",

}

> AGENT

Pushed `4b18949` to the PR.

## Addressed 5 actionable + 3 quick-win nitpicks

| Comment | Fix |
|---|---|
| `submit()` lacks state guard | `WHERE id=? AND status='pending'` + `.returning()` check, throws `VersionStateError` |
| `recordScanResult()` lacks state guard | Pre-reads status, throws if not `scanning`; scan-bundle catches and no-ops (idempotent Inngest retries) |
| `publishVersion()` lacks ready check | Pre-reads status; throws unless `ready` |
| `isFirstInstall` race | Prior-install lookup moved inside the tx |
| docs/api.md stale | Updated to new response shape |
| Non-deterministic latest | Added `desc(createdAt)` tiebreaker |
| Silent rethrow → 500 | Catch-all returns `jsonError(500, "internal_error")` |
| Tests don't assert `where` | Added `.where` call assertions to each lifecycle test |

## Skipped with reasons

- "Drop `latest` sentinel from request schema" — contract change, low payoff for v0.1.
- "Avoid non-null assertion" — addressed indirectly (the `version!` is gone because `resolvedVersion` is now declared after the resolution branch).
- "Validate explicit version exists for the extension" — bearer auth gates this; recording an unknown version is harmless garbage, not a security concern.

143 tests pass (was 139; 4 new tests cover the state-guard throw paths). The state-guard work is the deeper version of the original refactor — without those preconditions the module wasn't really a state machine. CodeRabbit's review surfaced that.

> DEVELOPER

  "description": "Reply to recordScanResult guard comment"
}

Returns the\n+// extension id so the caller can revalidate caches and dispatch\n+// downstream events (those are orchestration, not state).\n+//\n+// Note: extensions.search_vector is a GENERATED column maintained by\n+// Postgres; this module never writes to it.\n+export async function publishVersion(\n+  versionId: string,\n+): Promise<{ extensionId: string }> {\n+  const [row] = await db\n+ VersionStateError(\"version_not_found\");\n+  const { extensionId } = row;\n+  const now = new Date();\n+\n+  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n+    await tx\n+      .update(extensions)\n+      .set({ visibility: \"published\", publishedAt: now })\n+      .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId));\n+    await tx\n+      .update(extensionVersions)\n+      .set({ publishedAt: now })\n+      .where(eq(extensionVersions.id, versionId));\n+  });","path":"lib/extensions/state.ts","commit_id":"4b189491680073c2ccf148cf5181ee66a657ffb7","original_commit_id":"bc95892612b477a4ccfb301c57c76cba47f933f5","user":{"login":"hutusi","id":487036,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjQ4NzAzNg==","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/487036?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi","html_url":"https://github.com/hutusi","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/hutusi/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Done   "command": "gh api repos/ainaive/agentcenter/pulls/4/comments/3205707400/replies -f body=\"Done in 4b18949. The prior-install lookup is now inside the transaction (\\`tx.select(...)\\` followed by the writes). Both reads see the same snapshot.\n\nHonest caveat: under READ COMMITTED, two concurrent transactions for the same \\`(userId, extensionId)\\` can still both observe \\`prior.length === 0\\`. True race-safety would need either a unique constraint or \\`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE\\` (and the same pattern would extend to atomic dedupe, which we don't currently want under the (b) decision in the design discussion: every install records). I'm happy to follow up with stricter isolation if you'd like — for now \\`isFirstInstall\\` is best-effort, but the move closes the larger window.\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Reply to isFirstInstall race comment"
}

\"@/lib/db/schema/collection\";\n+import { extensions, extensionVersions } from \"@/lib/db/schema/extension\";\n+import { getOrCreateSystemCollection } from \"@/lib/db/queries/collections\";\n+\n+export type ExtensionRef = { id: string } | { slug: string };\n+export type InstallSource = \"web\" | \"cli\";\n+\n+export type InstallErrorCode = \"extension_not_found\" | \"no_published_version\";\n+\n+export class InstallError extends Error {\n+  readonly code: InstallErrorCode;\n+  constructor(code: InstallErrorCode) {\n+    super(code);\n+    this.code = code;\n+    this.name = \"InstallError\";\n+  }\n+}\n+\n+export interface RecordInstallParams {\n+  userId: string;\n+  extension: ExtensionRef;\n+  source: InstallSource;\n+  version?: string;\n+}\n+\n+export interface InstallRecord {\n+  installId: string;\n+  isFirstInstall: boolean;\n+  version: string;\n+}\n+\n+export async function recordInstall(\n+  params: RecordInstallParams,\n+): Promise<InstallRecord> {\n+  const { userId, extension, source } = params;\n+\n+  const extWhere =\n+    \"id\" in extension\n+      ? eq(extensions.id, extension.id)\n+      : eq(extensions.slug, extension.slug);\n+\n+  const [ext] = await db\n+    .select({ id: extensions.id })\n+    .from(extensions)\n+    .where(extWhere)\n+    .limit(1);\n+\n+  if (!ext) throw new InstallError(\"extension_not_found\");\n+  const extensionId = ext.id;\n+\n+  let version = params.version;\n+  if (!version) {\n+    const [latest] = await db\n+      .select({ version: extensionVersions.version })\n+      .from(extensionVersions)\n+      .where(\n+        and(\n+          eq(extensionVersions.extensionId, extensionId),\n+          eq(extensionVersions.status, \"ready\"),\n+        ),\n+      )\n+      .orderBy(desc(extensionVersions.publishedAt))\n+      .limit(1);\n+    if (!latest) throw new InstallError(\"no_published_version\");\n+    version = latest.version;\n+  }\n+\n+  const installedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(userId, \"installed\");\n+\n+  const prior = await db\n+    .select({ id: installs.id })\n+    .from(installs)\n+    .where(and(eq(installs.userId, userId), eq(installs.extensionId, extensionId)))\n+    .limit(1);\n+  const isFirstInstall = prior.length === 0;\n+\n+  const installId = crypto.randomUUID();\n+\n+  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {\n+    await tx.insert(installs).values({\n+      id: installId,\n+      userId,\n+      extensionId,\n+      version: version!,\n+      source,\n+    });\n+\n+    await tx\n+      .insert(collectionItems)\n+      .values({ collectionId: installedColId, extensionId })\n+      .onConflictDoNothing();\n+\n+    await tx\n+      .update(extensions)\n+      .set({ in 4b18949. The prior-install lookup is now inside the transaction (`tx.select(...)` followed by the writes). Both reads see the same snapshot.\n\nHonest caveat: under READ COMMITTED, two concurrent transactions for the same `(userId, extensionId)` can still both observe `prior.length === 0`. True race-safety would need either a unique constraint or `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (and the same pattern would extend to atomic dedupe, which we don't currently want under the (b) decision in the design discussion: every install records).      171 /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/docs/manifest-spec.md
# AgentCenter Extension Manifest Spec

Every extension bundle (`.zip`) **must** contain a `manifest.toml` at the archive root.
The manifest declares identity, discoverability, compatibility, and per-agent install targets.

---

## Top-level sections

### `[extension]` — required

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slug` | string | ✓ | URL-safe identifier, unique across the registry. Pattern: `[a-z0-9-]+`, max 64 chars. |
| `name` | string | ✓ | Display name in English. |
| `nameZh` | string | | Display name in Chinese (optional bilingual). |
| `version` | string | ✓ | Semver (e.g. `1.0.0`). |
| `category` | enum | ✓ | One of `skills`, `mcp`, `slash`, `plugins`. |
| `scope` | enum | ✓ | Audience: `personal`, `org`, `enterprise`. |
| `license` | string | | SPDX identifier (e.g. `MIT`, `Apache-2.0`). Omit for proprietary. |
| `description` | string | ✓ | Short description in English, max 280 chars. |
| `descriptionZh` | string | | Short description in Chinese. |
| `tagline` | string | | One-line tagline shown in the featured banner. |
| `tags` | string[] | | Tag slugs from the registry vocabulary (see Tags below). |

### `[categorization]` — required

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `funcCat` | enum | Functional category: `workTask`, `business`, `tools`. |
| `subCat` | string | L1 subcategory key (e.g. `softDev`, `systemDesign`). |
| `l2` | string | L2 sub-subcategory key (e.g. `backend`, `frontend`). Optional. |

### `[links]` — optional

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `homepage` | URL | Public homepage or docs site. |
| `repo` | URL | Source repository (GitHub, GitLab, etc.). |

### `[compatibility]` — optional

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `minAgentVersion` | string | Minimum agent version required (semver, e.g. `0.8.0`). |
| `os` | string[] | Restrict to OS list: `darwin`, `linux`, `windows`. Omit = all. |

### `[install.<agent>]` — at least one required

Declares per-agent install destinations. The CLI reads the profile that matches
`agentcenter config get agent` (default: `claude`).

**Skills** (`category = "skills"`):

```toml
[install.claude]
skills = "~/.claude/skills/{slug}"
```

**MCP Servers** (`category = "mcp"`):

```toml
[install.claude]
mcpConfig = "~/.claude/claude_desktop_config.json"
mcpKey    = "{slug}"
```

**Slash Commands** (`category = "slash"`):

```toml
[install.claude]
commands = "~/.claude/commands/{slug}.md"
```

**Plugins** (`category = "plugins"`):

```toml
[install.claude]
plugins = "~/.claude/plugins/{slug}"
```
```

> AGENT

Good news: there's a `docs/manifest-spec.md` that's the canonical source of truth (171 lines). Both existing schemas have *each* drifted from it. Concrete catalog:

| Field | Spec | Form (`manifest.ts`) | Bundle (`scan-bundle.ts`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `slug` | regex + max 64 | regex + min 3 + max **80** | min 1 + max **64** + no regex |
| `name` | (no length) | min 2 + max 80 | **min 1** |
| `description` | max 280 | max **300**, optional | max 280, **required** |
| `version` | semver | semver | semver |
| `tagline` | (allowed) | max 120 | **missing** |
| `nameZh` / `descriptionZh` | allowed | optional | **missing** |

Three drifts at once: form↔spec, bundle↔spec, form↔bundle. Each accepts data the other rejects.

### Proposed design (one schema module)

`lib/validators/extension-manifest.ts` (replaces `manifest.ts`) exports three schemas built on shared primitives:

```ts
// Single source of truth — matches docs/manifest-spec.md exactly.
export const ExtensionManifestCore = z.object({
  slug:        z.string().min(3).max(64).regex(SLUG_RE, "..."),
  name:        z.string().min(2).max(80),
  nameZh:      z.string().max(80).optional(),
  version:     z.string().regex(SEMVER_RE, "..."),
  category:    z.enum(["skills", "mcp", "slash", "plugins"]),
  scope:       z.enum(["personal", "org", "enterprise"]),
  funcCat:     z.enum(["workTask", "business", "tools"]),
  subCat:      z.string().min(1).max(60),
  l2:          z.string().max(60).optional(),
  description: z.string().max(280).optional(),
  descriptionZh: z.string().max(280).optional(),
  tagline:     z.string().max(120).optional(),
});

// Form: core + UI-only fields.
export const FormManifestSchema = ExtensionManifestCore.extend({
  tagIds:      z.array(z.string()).max(8),
  deptId:      z.string().optional(),
  homepageUrl: z.string().url().optional().or(z.literal("")),
  repoUrl:     z.string().url().optional().or(z.literal("")),
  licenseSpdx: z.string().max(40).optional(),
});

// Bundle: core lifted into TOML sections.
export const BundleManifestSchema = z.object({
  extension:     ExtensionManifestCore.pick({ slug, name, nameZh, version, category, scope, description, descriptionZh, tagline }),
  categorization: ExtensionManifestCore.pick({ funcCat, subCat, l2 }),
});
```

### Two decisions worth grilling

**Q1. Tighten the bundle?**

Today's bundle schema is more permissive than the form (no slug regex, `name.min(1)`, description required at max 280). Adopting `ExtensionManifestCore` *tightens* the bundle: a publisher whose `manifest.toml` had `name = "x"` (1 char) used to scan-pass and would now fail. That's the point — we're catching what the form has been catching all along. But if there are real published bundles in production already, this could reject them on re-scan.

- **(a)** Tighten — the canonical is the truth, bundles meet it.
- **(b)** Keep bundle permissive — relax `min(1)`, no slug regex, optional description. The bundle gets a *looser* derived schema that opts out of some constraints.

I'd pick (a). Catching weak data at scan-time is better than letting it propagate. v0.1 probably has zero bundles in the wild yet. Push back if you know otherwise.

**Q2. Description: optional or required?**

Form has `description.optional()`. Bundle has `description` required. Spec table marks it ✓ (required).

- **(a)** Required everywhere — match the spec. Form gets a small change; users must write a description to publish.
- **(b)** Optional everywhere — looser. Bundle relaxes.
- **(c)** Different in form vs bundle (today's drifted state, codified).

(a) is the spec's intent. Form change is real but minor — anyone publishing should describe their thing.

Other field divergences (slug max, description length) all resolve to "match the spec, no decision needed."

---

**Pick (a/b) for Q1 and (a/b/c) for Q2.** Sketching tasks now; will execute once you commit.

> DEVELOPER

Q1: a Q2: a

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/validators/manifest.ts",
  "content": "import { z } from \"zod\";\n\n// Single source of truth for what makes an extension manifest valid.\n// Mirrors `docs/manifest-spec.md`. Form and bundle schemas below derive\n// from this core; nothing should redefine these constraints elsewhere.\nexport const ExtensionManifestCore = z.object({\n  slug: z\n    .string()\n    .min(3)\n    .max(64)\n    .regex(/^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/, \"Lowercase letters, numbers and hyphens only\"),\n  name: z.string().min(2).max(80),\n  nameZh: z.string().max(80).optional(),\n  version: z.string().regex(/^\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+$/, \"Must be semver (e.g. 1.0.0)\"),\n  category: z.enum([\"skills\", \"mcp\", \"slash\", \"plugins\"]),\n  scope: z.enum([\"personal\", \"org\", \"enterprise\"]),\n  funcCat: z.enum([\"workTask\", \"business\", \"tools\"]),\n  subCat: z.string().min(1).max(60),\n  l2: z.string().max(60).optional(),\n  description: z.string().min(1).max(280),\n  descriptionZh: z.string().max(280).optional(),\n  tagline: z.string().max(120).optional(),\n});\n\n// Form: every core field plus the UI-only extras the publish wizard collects.\nexport const ManifestFormSchema = ExtensionManifestCore.extend({\n  tagIds: z.array(z.string()).max(8),\n  deptId: z.string().optional(),\n  homepageUrl: z.string().url().optional().or(z.literal(\"\")),\n  repoUrl: z.string().url().optional().or(z.literal(\"\")),\n  licenseSpdx: z.string().max(40).optional(),\n});\n\nexport type ManifestFormValues = z.infer<typeof ManifestFormSchema>;\n\n// Bundle: same fields, lifted into the [extension] / [categorization] TOML\n// sections defined in docs/manifest-spec.md.\nexport const BundleManifestSchema = z.object({\n  extension: ExtensionManifestCore.pick({\n    slug: true,\n    name: true,\n    nameZh: true,\n    version: true,\n    category: true,\n    scope: true,\n    description: true,\n    descriptionZh: true,\n    tagline: true,\n  }),\n  categorization: ExtensionManifestCore.pick({\n    funcCat: true,\n    subCat: true,\n    l2: true,\n  }),\n});\n\nexport type BundleManifest = z.infer<typeof BundleManifestSchema>;\n"
}
