1	"use client";
2	
3	import { useState } from "react";
4	import { useTranslations } from "next-intl";
5	
6	import { TAG_LABELS } from "@/lib/tags";
7	import { DEPARTMENTS } from "@/lib/data/departments";
8	import type { ManifestFormValues } from "@/lib/validators/manifest";
9	import type { Department } from "@/types";
10	
11	interface Props {
12	  onSubmit: (values: ManifestFormValues) => void;
13	  defaultValues?: Partial<ManifestFormValues>;
14	}
15	
16	function flattenDepts(list: Department[], depth = 0): { id: string; label: string }[] {
17	  const out: { id: string; label: string }[] = [];
18	  for (const d of list) {
19	    out.push({ id: d.id, label: "  ".repeat(depth) + d.name });
20	    if (d.children) out.push(...flattenDepts(d.children, depth + 1));
21	  }
22	  return out;
23	}
24	
25	const DEPT_OPTIONS = flattenDepts(DEPARTMENTS);
26	const TAG_OPTIONS = Object.entries(TAG_LABELS).map(([id, label]) => ({ id, label: label.en }));
27	
28	export function ManifestForm({ onSubmit, defaultValues }: Props) {
29	  const t = useTranslations("publish.form");
30	  const tw = useTranslations("publish.wizard");
31	
32	  const [values, setValues] = useState<ManifestFormValues>({
33	    slug: "",
34	    name: "",
35	    nameZh: "",
36	    version: "1.0.0",
37	    category: "skills",
38	    scope: "personal",
39	    funcCat: "workTask",
40	    subCat: "",
41	    l2: "",
42	    deptId: "",
43	    tagIds: [],
44	    description: "",
45	    descriptionZh: "",
46	    tagline: "",
47	    homepageUrl: "",
48	    repoUrl: "",
49	    licenseSpdx: "",
50	    ...defaultValues,
51	  });
52	  const [errors, setErrors] = useState<Partial<Record<keyof ManifestFormValues, string>>>({});
53	
54	  function field(key: keyof ManifestFormValues) {
55	    return {
56	      value: String(values[key] ?? ""),
57	      onChange: (e: React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement | HTMLTextAreaElement | HTMLSelectElement>) =>
58	        setValues((v) => ({ ...v, [key]: e.target.value })),
59	    };
60	  }
61	
62	  function validate(): boolean {
63	    const errs: typeof errors = {};
64	    if (!values.slug) errs.slug = "Required";
65	    else if (!/^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/.test(values.slug)) errs.slug = t("slug");
66	    if (!values.name) errs.name = "Required";
67	    if (!values.version || !/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/.test(values.version)) errs.version = t("versionHint");
68	    if (!values.subCat) errs.subCat = "Required";
69	    setErrors(errs);
70	    return Object.keys(errs).length === 0;
71	  }
72	
73	  function handleSubmit(e: React.FormEvent) {
74	    e.preventDefault();
75	    if (validate()) onSubmit(values);
76	  }
77	
78	  function toggleTag(id: string) {
79	    setValues((v) => ({
80	      ...v,
81	      tagIds: v.tagIds.includes(id)
82	        ? v.tagIds.filter((t) => t !== id)
83	        : v.tagIds.length < 8
84	          ? [...v.tagIds, id]
85	          : v.tagIds,
86	    }));
87	  }
88	
89	  const inputCls =
90	    "w-full rounded-md border border-border bg-background px-3 py-2 text-sm focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-ring";
91	  const labelCls = "block text-sm font-medium text-muted-foreground mb-1";
92	  const errorCls = "mt-1 text-xs text-destructive";
93	
94	  return (
95	    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit} className="space-y-5">
96	      {/* Slug + Version */}
97	      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4">
98	        <div>
99	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("slug")} *</label>
100	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("slug")} placeholder="my-extension" />
101	          {errors.slug && <p className={errorCls}>{errors.slug}</p>}
102	          <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">{t("slugHint")}</p>
103	        </div>
104	        <div>
105	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("version")} *</label>
106	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("version")} placeholder="1.0.0" />
107	          {errors.version && <p className={errorCls}>{errors.version}</p>}
108	        </div>
109	      </div>
110	
111	      {/* Name EN + ZH */}
112	      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4">
113	        <div>
114	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("name")} *</label>
115	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("name")} />
116	          {errors.name && <p className={errorCls}>{errors.name}</p>}
117	        </div>
118	        <div>
119	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("nameZh")}</label>
120	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("nameZh")} />
121	        </div>
122	      </div>
123	
124	      {/* Category + Scope */}
125	      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4">
126	        <div>
127	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("category")} *</label>
128	          <select className={inputCls} {...field("category")}>
129	            {(["skills", "mcp", "slash", "plugins"] as const).map((c) => (
130	              <option key={c} value={c}>{c}</option>
131	            ))}
132	          </select>
133	        </div>
134	        <div>
135	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("scope")} *</label>
136	          <select className={inputCls} {...field("scope")}>
137	            {(["personal", "org", "enterprise"] as const).map((s) => (
138	              <option key={s} value={s}>{s}</option>
139	            ))}
140	          </select>
141	        </div>
142	      </div>
143	
144	      {/* FuncCat + SubCat + L2 */}
145	      <div className="grid grid-cols-3 gap-4">
146	        <div>
147	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("funcCat")} *</label>
148	          <select className={inputCls} {...field("funcCat")}>
149	            {(["workTask", "business", "tools"] as const).map((f) => (
150	              <option key={f} value={f}>{f}</option>
151	            ))}
152	          </select>
153	        </div>
154	        <div>
155	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("subCat")} *</label>
156	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("subCat")} placeholder="e.g. search" />
157	          {errors.subCat && <p className={errorCls}>{errors.subCat}</p>}
158	        </div>
159	        <div>
160	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("l2")}</label>
161	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("l2")} />
162	        </div>
163	      </div>
164	
165	      {/* Dept */}
166	      <div>
167	        <label className={labelCls}>{t("dept")}</label>
168	        <select className={inputCls} {...field("deptId")}>
169	          <option value="">— none —</option>
170	          {DEPT_OPTIONS.map((d) => (
171	            <option key={d.id} value={d.id}>{d.label}</option>
172	          ))}
173	        </select>
174	      </div>
175	
176	      {/* Tags */}
177	      <div>
178	        <label className={labelCls}>{t("tags")} <span className="text-muted-foreground font-normal">({t("tagsHint")})</span></label>
179	        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-2 mt-1">
180	          {TAG_OPTIONS.map(({ id, label }) => {
181	            const active = values.tagIds.includes(id);
182	            return (
183	              <button
184	                key={id}
185	                type="button"
186	                onClick={() => toggleTag(id)}
187	                className={`rounded-full px-3 py-1 text-xs border transition-colors ${
188	                  active
189	                    ? "bg-primary text-primary-foreground border-primary"
190	                    : "border-border text-muted-foreground hover:border-primary"
191	                }`}
192	              >
193	                {label}
194	              </button>
195	            );
196	          })}
197	        </div>
198	      </div>
199	
200	      {/* Tagline */}
201	      <div>
202	        <label className={labelCls}>{t("tagline")}</label>
203	        <input className={inputCls} {...field("tagline")} maxLength={120} />
204	      </div>
205	
206	      {/* Descriptions */}
207	      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4">
208	        <div>
209	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("description")}</label>
210	          <textarea className={inputCls} {...field("description")} rows={3} maxLength={300} />
211	        </div>
212	        <div>
213	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("descriptionZh")}</label>
214	          <textarea className={inputCls} {...field("descriptionZh")} rows={3} maxLength={300} />
215	        </div>
216	      </div>
217	
218	      {/* Links */}
219	      <div className="grid grid-cols-3 gap-4">
220	        <div>
221	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("homepageUrl")}</label>
222	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("homepageUrl")} type="url" placeholder="https://" />
223	        </div>
224	        <div>
225	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("repoUrl")}</label>
226	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("repoUrl")} type="url" placeholder="https://github.com/…" />
227	        </div>
228	        <div>
229	          <label className={labelCls}>{t("licenseSpdx")}</label>
230	          <input className={inputCls} {...field("licenseSpdx")} placeholder="MIT" />
231	        </div>
232	      </div>
233	
234	      <div className="pt-2 flex justify-end">
235	        <button
236	          type="submit"
237	          className="rounded-md bg-primary px-5 py-2 text-sm font-medium text-primary-foreground hover:opacity-90 transition-opacity"
238	        >
239	          {tw("nextButton")}
240	        </button>
241	      </div>
242	    </form>
243	  );
244	}
245

1	// @vitest-environment happy-dom
2	import { describe, it, expect, vi, beforeEach, afterEach } from "vitest";
3	import { render, screen, waitFor, cleanup } from "@testing-library/react";
4	import userEvent from "@testing-library/user-event";
5	
6	import { ManifestForm } from "./manifest-form";
7	
8	vi.mock("next-intl", () => ({
9	  useTranslations: () => (key: string) => key,
10	}));
11	
12	afterEach(cleanup);
13	
14	const VALID_DEFAULTS = {
15	  slug: "my-skill",
16	  name: "My Skill",
17	  subCat: "search",
18	};
19	
20	describe("ManifestForm", () => {
21	  let user: ReturnType<typeof userEvent.setup>;
22	
23	  beforeEach(() => {
24	    user = userEvent.setup();
25	  });
26	
27	  it("renders the form with slug placeholder and default version", () => {
28	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} />);
29	    expect(screen.getByPlaceholderText("my-extension")).toBeInTheDocument();
30	    expect(screen.getByDisplayValue("1.0.0")).toBeInTheDocument();
31	  });
32	
33	  it("shows Required errors on submit when slug, name, and subCat are empty", async () => {
34	    const onSubmit = vi.fn();
35	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={onSubmit} />);
36	
37	    await user.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "nextButton" }));
38	
39	    const errors = await screen.findAllByText("Required");
40	    expect(errors).toHaveLength(3); // slug, name, subCat
41	    expect(onSubmit).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
42	  });
43	
44	  it("shows a slug format error for a slug that fails the regex", async () => {
45	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} />);
46	
47	    await user.type(screen.getByPlaceholderText("my-extension"), "Invalid Slug!");
48	    await user.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "nextButton" }));
49	
50	    // t("slug") returns the key "slug" — that is the error message the component sets
51	    await waitFor(() => expect(screen.getByText("slug")).toBeInTheDocument());
52	  });
53	
54	  it("calls onSubmit with the form values when all required fields are valid", async () => {
55	    const onSubmit = vi.fn();
56	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={onSubmit} defaultValues={VALID_DEFAULTS} />);
57	
58	    await user.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "nextButton" }));
59	
60	    await waitFor(() => expect(onSubmit).toHaveBeenCalledOnce());
61	    expect(onSubmit).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
62	      expect.objectContaining({
63	        slug: "my-skill",
64	        name: "My Skill",
65	        subCat: "search",
66	        version: "1.0.0",
67	        category: "skills",
68	      }),
69	    );
70	  });
71	
72	  it("adds a tag when its button is clicked", async () => {
73	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} />);
74	
75	    const searchTag = screen.getByRole("button", { name: "search" });
76	    expect(searchTag.className).not.toContain("bg-primary");
77	
78	    await user.click(searchTag);
79	    expect(searchTag.className).toContain("bg-primary");
80	  });
81	
82	  it("removes a tag when clicked a second time", async () => {
83	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} />);
84	
85	    const searchTag = screen.getByRole("button", { name: "search" });
86	    await user.click(searchTag);
87	    await user.click(searchTag);
88	
89	    expect(searchTag.className).not.toContain("bg-primary");
90	  });
91	
92	  it("does not add a 9th tag when 8 are already selected", async () => {
93	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} />);
94	
95	    // Click the first 9 tag buttons (all are type="button")
96	    const tagBtns = screen
97	      .getAllByRole("button")
98	      .filter((b) => b.getAttribute("type") === "button");
99	
100	    for (const btn of tagBtns.slice(0, 9)) {
101	      await user.click(btn);
102	    }
103	
104	    const selected = tagBtns.filter((b) => b.className.includes("bg-primary"));
105	    expect(selected).toHaveLength(8);
106	  });
107	
108	  it("pre-populates fields from defaultValues", () => {
109	    render(<ManifestForm onSubmit={vi.fn()} defaultValues={{ slug: "pre-filled", version: "2.0.0" }} />);
110	
111	    expect(screen.getByDisplayValue("pre-filled")).toBeInTheDocument();
112	    expect(screen.getByDisplayValue("2.0.0")).toBeInTheDocument();
113	  });
114	});
115

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"refactor(manifest): single source of truth for manifest validation\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n`lib/validators/manifest.ts` (form) and `lib/jobs/scan-bundle.ts` (bundle) each defined their own zod schema for what counts as a valid extension manifest. Both had drifted from `docs/manifest-spec.md` and from each other:\n\n| Field | Spec | Form (was) | Bundle (was) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `slug` | regex + max 64 | regex + max **80** | min 1 + max 64 + **no regex** |\n| `name` | — | min 2 + max 80 | **min 1** |\n| `description` | max 280, ✓ required | max **300**, **optional** | max 280, ✓ required |\n| `tagline`, `nameZh`, `descriptionZh` | allowed | optional | **missing** |\n\nResult: a bundle whose `manifest.toml` would have failed the publish form passed the scanner silently.\n\n## Design\n\n`lib/validators/manifest.ts` now exports `ExtensionManifestCore` — a single zod schema mirroring `docs/manifest-spec.md`. The two consumer-facing schemas derive from it:\n\n- **`ManifestFormSchema`** = `core.extend({ tagIds, deptId, homepageUrl, repoUrl, licenseSpdx })` (UI-only fields the wizard collects).\n- **`BundleManifestSchema`** = the same core fields lifted into `{ extension: ..., categorization: ... }` TOML sections via `.pick()`.\n\n`scan-bundle.ts` imports the bundle schema instead of redefining it. The shared `SLUG_PATTERN` / `SEMVER_PATTERN` constants are exported so the form's hand-rolled `validate()` doesn't fork the regex.\n\n## Behavioural changes\n\n- **Bundle validation tightens to the spec** — slug regex, `name.min(2)`, `description.min(1)`. Bundles that were silently accepted before (e.g. 1-char names, missing description) are now rejected at scan time. v0.1 has no production bundles yet, so this is a forward-looking tightening.\n- **Form description becomes required** to match the spec. `manifest-form.tsx` surfaces the inline error; the textarea `maxLength` changes 300 → 280.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run lint` — clean\n- [x] `bun run typecheck` — clean\n- [x] `bun run test` — 148 tests pass (143 → 148; 7 new tests on the shared core, bundle shape, and the new drift-fix invariants — slug max 64, description required, name min 2)\n- [ ] CI runs on this PR\n- [ ] Smoke-check the publish wizard: empty description should now block submission with an inline error, valid manifest should still publish end-to-end\n- [ ] Smoke-check that an existing test bundle (if any) still scans cleanly under the tightened schema\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Open PR"
}

<summary>🧹 Nitpick comments (1)</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>components/publish/manifest-form.tsx (1)</summary><blockquote>

`66-76`: _💤 Low value_

**`validate()` doesn't enforce `slug.min(3)` or `name.min(2)` from the schema.**

`SLUG_PATTERN` matches a single character (e.g., `"a"`), so slugs of 1–2 chars silently pass the form's regex check but will fail `ManifestFormSchema.safeParse()` downstream. Similarly, `name: "x"` passes the truthy `!values.name` guard but fails `min(2)`. Adding the `minLength` HTML attribute (and/or explicit checks) would surface the error immediately.

<details>
<summary>✏️ Suggested additions</summary>

```diff
  if (!values.slug) errs.slug = "Required";
  else if (!SLUG_PATTERN.test(values.slug)) errs.slug = t("slug");
+ else if (values.slug.length < 3) errs.slug = "Required";
  if (!values.name) errs.name = "Required";
+ else if (values.name.length < 2) errs.name = "Required";
```

```diff
- <input className={inputCls} {...field("slug")} placeholder="my-extension" maxLength={64} />
+ <input className={inputCls} {...field("slug")} placeholder="my-extension" minLength={3} maxLength={64} />

> AGENT

CodeRabbit posted just one nitpick (no actionable issues, no major concerns):

**Form's `validate()` lets short slugs and 1-char names slip through to the schema.** `SLUG_PATTERN` matches 1+ chars; the form has `!values.name` (truthy) check. So a slug `"ab"` or name `"x"` passes form validation, then fails `ManifestFormSchema.safeParse()` server-side. UX delay, not a bug.

Worth fixing — small UX win, mirrors what the schema already enforces. I'll add the length checks + `minLength` HTML attributes + a quick test. Going.

> AGENT

Browser-side `minLength` blocks the form submit (happy-dom enforces it), so the JS `validate()` never runs. Adding `noValidate` to the form keeps the HTML attributes as visual hints but lets the JS path handle errors.

> AGENT

Pushed `4108329` and posted a reply on PR #6.

CodeRabbit had only one nitpick (no inline comments, no actionable items — clean review). Fix:

- `validate()` now enforces `slug.length >= 3` and `name.length >= 2` with explicit error messages.
- Added `minLength`/`maxLength` HTML attributes on the slug and name inputs as visual cues.
- Added `noValidate` to the `<form>` element — without it, happy-dom (and real browsers) would block the submit at minLength before the JS path runs, hiding our error messages behind a generic browser tooltip.
- Two new tests cover the length-error paths.

150 tests pass (was 148; 2 new). Branch is ready to merge when you are.

1	import { z } from "zod";
2	
3	const CATEGORIES = ["skills", "mcp", "slash", "plugins"] as const;
4	const SCOPES = ["personal", "org", "enterprise"] as const;
5	const FUNC_CATS = ["workTask", "business", "tools"] as const;
6	const FILTER_CHIPS = ["all", "trending", "new", "official", "free"] as const;
7	const SORTS = ["downloads", "stars", "recent"] as const;
8	const TAG_MATCHES = ["any", "all"] as const;
9	
10	export const PAGE_SIZE = 24;
11	
12	export const filtersSchema = z.object({
13	  q: z.string().trim().min(1).max(120).optional(),
14	  category: z.enum(CATEGORIES).optional(),
15	  scope: z.enum(SCOPES).optional(),
16	  funcCat: z.enum(FUNC_CATS).optional(),
17	  subCat: z.string().trim().min(1).max(40).optional(),
18	  l2: z.string().trim().min(1).max(40).optional(),
19	  // Department id (dotted-path) or the literal "__all" to disable the filter.
20	  dept: z.string().trim().min(1).max(120).optional(),
21	  tags: z.array(z.string().trim().min(1).max(40)).max(16).optional(),
22	  tagMatch: z.enum(TAG_MATCHES).optional(),
23	  filter: z.enum(FILTER_CHIPS).optional(),
24	  sort: z.enum(SORTS).optional(),
25	  page: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(1000).optional(),
26	});
27	
28	export type Filters = z.infer<typeof filtersSchema>;
29	
30	/**
31	 * Parse Next.js searchParams into typed Filters. Invalid input is dropped,
32	 * so a malformed URL silently falls back to "no filter" rather than 500ing.
33	 */
34	export function parseFilters(
35	  input: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>,
36	): Filters {
37	  const tags = input.tags;
38	  const normalized = {
39	    ...input,
40	    tags:
41	      tags === undefined
42	        ? undefined
43	        : Array.isArray(tags)
44	          ? tags
45	          : tags.split(",").filter(Boolean),
46	  };
47	  const parsed = filtersSchema.safeParse(normalized);
48	  return parsed.success ? parsed.data : {};
49	}
50	
51	export function pageOffset(page: number | undefined) {
52	  return ((page ?? 1) - 1) * PAGE_SIZE;
53	}
54

1	import { and, desc, eq, ilike, inArray, like, or, type SQL, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
2	
3	import { MY_DEPT_ID } from "@/lib/data/departments"; // fallback for unauthenticated users
4	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
5	import { extensions, extensionTags } from "@/lib/db/schema";
6	import type { Filters } from "@/lib/validators/filters";
7	
8	// Special token in the dept URL param meaning "show all departments".
9	const ALL_DEPTS = "__all";
10	
11	/**
12	 * Compose a SQL WHERE expression from typed filters. Returns `undefined` when
13	 * no clauses are active so callers can omit `.where()` entirely.
14	 */
15	export function buildExtensionWhere(
16	  filters: Filters,
17	  fallbackDeptId?: string,
18	): SQL | undefined {
19	  const clauses = [
20	    // Always limit to published extensions.
21	    eq(extensions.visibility, "published"),
22	
23	    filters.category ? eq(extensions.category, filters.category) : undefined,
24	    filters.scope ? eq(extensions.scope, filters.scope) : undefined,
25	    filters.funcCat ? eq(extensions.funcCat, filters.funcCat) : undefined,
26	    filters.subCat ? eq(extensions.subCat, filters.subCat) : undefined,
27	    filters.l2 ? eq(extensions.l2, filters.l2) : undefined,
28	
29	    // Dotted-path descendant filter — uses idx_ext_dept_path (text_pattern_ops).
30	    // When no dept param is provided, fall back to the authenticated user's
31	    // defaultDeptId (passed in from the page via getSession), then to MY_DEPT_ID
32	    // for unauthenticated visitors.
33	    (() => {
34	      const dept = filters.dept ?? fallbackDeptId ?? MY_DEPT_ID;
35	      if (dept === ALL_DEPTS) return undefined;
36	      return or(
37	        eq(extensions.deptId, dept),
38	        like(extensions.deptId, `${dept}.%`),
39	      );
40	    })(),
41	
42	    // Filter chips
43	    filters.filter === "trending"
44	      ? sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} > 50000`
45	      : undefined,
46	    filters.filter === "new" ? eq(extensions.badge, "new") : undefined,
47	    filters.filter === "official"
48	      ? eq(extensions.badge, "official")
49	      : undefined,
50	    filters.filter === "free"
51	      ? sql`${extensions.licenseSpdx} IS NOT NULL`
52	      : undefined,
53	
54	    // FTS via tsvector (set weight A/B/C in the GENERATED column) with
55	    // pg_trgm ILIKE fallback for short queries and CJK substrings.
56	    filters.q
57	      ? or(
58	          sql`${extensions.searchVector} @@ websearch_to_tsquery('simple', ${filters.q})`,
59	          ilike(extensions.name, `%${filters.q}%`),
60	          ilike(extensions.nameZh, `%${filters.q}%`),
61	        )
62	      : undefined,
63	
64	    // Tag filter via aggregating subquery — `any` matches if at least one tag
65	    // overlaps; `all` requires every selected tag to be present.
66	    filters.tags && filters.tags.length > 0
67	      ? inArray(
68	          extensions.id,
69	          db
70	            .select({ id: extensionTags.extensionId })
71	            .from(extensionTags)
72	            .where(inArray(extensionTags.tagId, filters.tags))
73	            .groupBy(extensionTags.extensionId)
74	            .having(
75	              filters.tagMatch === "all"
76	                ? sql`count(*) = ${filters.tags.length}`
77	                : sql`count(*) >= 1`,
78	            ),
79	        )
80	      : undefined,
81	  ].filter((c): c is SQL => Boolean(c));
82	
83	  return clauses.length > 0 ? and(...clauses) : undefined;
84	}
85	
86	export function buildExtensionOrder(sort: Filters["sort"]) {
87	  switch (sort) {
88	    case "stars":
89	      return [desc(extensions.starsAvg), desc(extensions.downloadsCount)];
90	    case "recent":
91	      return [desc(extensions.publishedAt), desc(extensions.createdAt)];
92	    case "downloads":
93	    default:
94	      return [desc(extensions.downloadsCount)];
95	  }
96	}
97

1	"use client";
2	
3	import { useSearchParams } from "next/navigation";
4	import { useTransition } from "react";
5	
6	import { usePathname, useRouter } from "@/lib/i18n/navigation";
7	
8	export function useFilterUrl() {
9	  const router = useRouter();
10	  const pathname = usePathname();
11	  const searchParams = useSearchParams();
12	  const [pending, startTransition] = useTransition();
13	
14	  function commit(params: URLSearchParams) {
15	    params.delete("page");
16	    const qs = params.toString();
17	    startTransition(() => {
18	      router.replace(
19	        qs ? `${pathname}?${qs}` : pathname,
20	        { scroll: false } as Parameters<typeof router.replace>[1],
21	      );
22	    });
23	  }
24	
25	  function set(key: string, value: string | null) {
26	    const params = new URLSearchParams(searchParams);
27	    if (value === null || value === "") {
28	      params.delete(key);
29	    } else {
30	      params.set(key, value);
31	    }
32	    commit(params);
33	  }
34	
35	  function setMulti(key: string, values: string[]) {
36	    const params = new URLSearchParams(searchParams);
37	    if (values.length === 0) {
38	      params.delete(key);
39	    } else {
40	      params.set(key, values.join(","));
41	    }
42	    commit(params);
43	  }
44	
45	  function get(key: string): string | null {
46	    return searchParams.get(key);
47	  }
48	
49	  function getMulti(key: string): string[] {
50	    const v = searchParams.get(key);
51	    return v ? v.split(",").filter(Boolean) : [];
52	  }
53	
54	  return { get, getMulti, set, setMulti, pending };
55	}
56	
```

> AGENT

Re-read the four files to get this right. The picture's a bit different from my initial sketch.

### What's actually there

- **`lib/validators/filters.ts`** — owns the `Filters` type via zod schema (11 fields: `q, category, scope, funcCat, subCat, l2, dept, tags, tagMatch, filter, sort, page`). Exports `parseFilters` (URL searchParams → Filters). **Earning its keep** by the deletion test.
- **`lib/search/query.ts`** — `buildExtensionWhere(filters, fallbackDeptId?)` and `buildExtensionOrder(sort)`. Filters → Drizzle SQL. **Earning its keep**.
- **`lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts`** — `{ get, getMulti, set, setMulti }`. **Generic, untyped, doesn't know about `Filters` at all.** Components call `set("category", "skills")` with literal strings.
- **`components/filters/*`** — 6 components, each calls `useFilterUrl()` and types out the key strings.

### The friction, precisely

It's not a depth problem — none of these are shallow. It's a **coupling-via-strings problem**:

1. The `Filters` type is the hub of the concept, but the URL projection (what each component does) is *untyped*. A component types `set("catgeory", x)` and the value silently drops on the next page render (because `parseFilters` rejects unknown keys). No crash, no test failure — just a filter chip that mysteriously doesn't work.
2. The comma-encoding for `tags` lives in two places: `parseFilters` splits on `","` (validators/filters.ts:45), `useFilterUrl.getMulti` splits on `","` (hooks/use-filter-url.ts:51). Same encoding convention, two locations.
3. Filter-default policy (the dept fallback) lives only on the SQL side (`query.ts:34`); the URL side has no notion. If a UI ever needs to *render* the effective dept, it has to re-implement that rule.

### What the deep version looks like

Keep validators/filters.ts and query.ts as-is. Replace the generic hook with a typed one:

```ts
// lib/hooks/use-filters.ts
export function useFilters(): {
  filters: Filters;
  set: <K extends keyof Filters>(key: K, value: Filters[K]) => void;
} { … }
```

Components stop using string keys. Type system catches typos. Comma encoding (and any future encoding rules) lives once, inside the hook.

### Honest assessment

**Cost**: rewrite the hook (~50 lines), update 6 components (each is small — sub-100 lines), add tests for the typed round-trip. Half a day.

**Payoff**: depends almost entirely on *whether the filter set is going to change*. If you add a new filter (e.g. "minStars", "language", "compatibility version") in the next quarter, this refactor pays back twice — once when adding the type, once when each component you wire it into is type-safe. If filters are frozen at the current 11, the refactor is a stylistic improvement with no measurable effect.

I still wouldn't pick it up on principle alone. But if the next product slice adds *any* filter dimension, doing this refactor first is the right move — you'll add the new filter into a typed hub instead of three string-keyed islands. So my real recommendation: defer until you know whether filters are growing.

> DEVELOPER

if it is worth to refactor, I would like do it now, not let the debt to be stay in the future. what do you think?

> AGENT

Honest pushback: this isn't really debt. Debt is when you took a shortcut knowing you'd pay later — none of those modules are shallow, none silently fail, and none are blocking a known change. It's a stylistic preference between two valid structures. Treating "valid alternative exists" as debt to clear pre-emptively means refactoring forever.

The rule I'd suggest instead of "don't accumulate debt": refactor when there's *evidence* — a bug, a planned change, a teammate confused by it, a measured slowdown. The filter setup has none of those today. Doing it now also costs review time (PR + your attention) and adds churn that future `git blame` has to walk through.

That said — if you want to ship it anyway, I'll do it. The work is small and the result is genuinely typed. Just say go and I'll grill the design.

> DEVELOPER

but you said it is good for the furture changed?

> AGENT

Fair catch — I was inconsistent. Let me untangle:

- "Good for future change" was conditional: *if* filters grow, this pays off. The refactor's value depends on whether that "if" fires.
- My pushback was a decision rule: don't refactor on speculation alone, wait for evidence.

Both can be true, but together they read as a flip-flop. The honest version: I don't know your roadmap; you do. If you have a real expectation filters will grow (any of: another category, language filter, compat-version filter, OS filter, "my dept only" toggle), the refactor pays back. If filters are frozen, it doesn't.

You're closer to that answer than I am. If your read is "filters will grow," I'll grill the design now. If not, defer.

> DEVELOPER

I think filters may grow, but not much, may be add one or two kinds of filters.

> AGENT

That's marginal — 1-2 filters isn't enough to justify the full typed-hook rewrite, but it's also not zero.

Concrete proposal: a **minimal refactor** instead of the full one. Add `keyof Filters` constraints to the existing `useFilterUrl` hook's `get`/`set`/`getMulti`/`setMulti`. Components keep their current call style (`set("category", "skills")`), but typos like `set("catgeory", ...)` become compile errors. About 10–15 lines of hook change, no component rewrites.

This captures the highest-value friction (silent string-key failures) for the smallest cost. The two-place comma encoding and the default-policy split stay as-is — they're real but small, and "1-2 new filters" doesn't make either bite. If filters grow more later, escalate to the full typed hook then.

Want me to do that minimal version? Or would you rather have the full typed-hook design grilled?

> DEVELOPER

I think full is better, if we refactor, we do it thoughtly. what do you think?

> AGENT

Locked: full typed hook. The design.

### Module layout

- **`lib/validators/filters.ts`** stays — adds a sibling `serializeFilters(filters: Partial<Filters>): URLSearchParams`. Both URL projections (parse + serialize) live next to the type. Comma-encoding lives once, here.
- **`lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts`** → renamed `lib/hooks/use-filters.ts`. Becomes thin: reads `useSearchParams()`, calls `parseFilters` to expose `filters: Filters`, exposes `update(partial)` and `pending`.
- **6 components** updated to `update({ key: value })` instead of `set("key", value)` / `setMulti("key", values)`.

### Hook surface

```ts
export function useFilters(): {
  filters: Filters;                                          // parsed, validated
  update: (partial: Partial<Filters>) => void;               // multi-key, typed
  pending: boolean;                                          // from useTransition
}
```

One method, not four. `update({ category: "skills" })` for the simple case; `update({ tags: [...current, "new"], tagMatch: "all" })` when you need atomicity. Removing a key: `update({ category: undefined })`.

### Behavior preserved

- **Page reset on filter change**: same as today (`commit` strips `page` unless you explicitly set it). Rule: if `partial.page === undefined`, drop `page` from URL; if you pass `page: 2`, keep it.
- **`router.replace` with `scroll: false` inside `useTransition`**: same.
- **Invalid URL state silently becomes empty Filters**: same — `parseFilters` already does this.

### Two design questions to grill

**Q1. Does the hook expose write-only too?** Some components only write (e.g. a "Clear filters" button) and don't need `filters`. Keeping a single `useFilters()` is simpler; readers ignore `filters`. Don't optimize for selective subscription unless renders are measurably slow.

I'd say **no separate writer** — one hook, callers ignore what they don't need. Push back if you'd rather have `useUpdateFilters()` separately.

**Q2. What does `update({ category: undefined })` mean — clear that filter, or no-op?**

- **(a) Clear** — explicit `undefined` removes the key from the URL. Symmetric: `update({ category: "skills" })` sets, `update({ category: undefined })` clears.
- **(b) No-op** — `undefined` means "don't touch this key." To clear, use a separate call like `clear("category")` or `update({ category: null as any })`.

(a) is more ergonomic; (b) is more explicit. JavaScript convention leans (a) — `Object.assign` treats undefined as "set." But TypeScript's `Partial<T>` includes undefined as a valid value, so (a) requires no type contortions.

I'd lock **(a)**.

---

**Sign off on the design + Q1=no, Q2=(a)?** If yes, I'll execute. The 6 components touched are: `filter-bar.tsx`, `filter-chips.tsx`, `scope-pills.tsx`, `dept-picker.tsx`, `sort-select.tsx`, `tag-drawer.tsx`. Each is a small change.

> DEVELOPER

I agree, go ahead

55	      {open && (
56	        <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-1.5 pt-1">
57	          {visible.map((t) => {
58	            const isActive = active.includes(t.id);
59	            return (
60	              <button
61	                key={t.id}
62	                type="button"
63	                onClick={() => toggle(t.id)}
64	                className={cn(
65	                  "inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 rounded-full border px-2.5 py-0.5 font-mono text-[11px] font-semibold transition",
66	                  isActive
67	                    ? "bg-primary text-primary-foreground border-primary"
68	                    : "border-border text-muted-foreground hover:border-primary hover:text-primary",
69	                )}
70	              >
71	                {t.id}
72	                <span
73	                  className={cn(
74	                    "text-[10px]",
75	                    isActive ? "opacity-70" : "opacity-50",
76	                  )}
77	                >
78	                  {t.count}
79	                </span>
80	              </button>
81	            );
82	          })}
83	
84	          {!showAll && hiddenCount > 0 && (
85	            <button
86	              type="button"
87	              onClick={() => setShowAll(true)}
88	              className="text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground text-[11.5px] font-semibold underline underline-offset-2 transition-colors"
89	            >
90	              +{hiddenCount} more
91	            </button>
92	          )}
93	          {showAll && hiddenCount > 0 && (
94	            <button
95	              type="button"
96	              onClick={() => setShowAll(false)}
97	              className="text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground text-[11.5px] font-semibold underline underline-offset-2 transition-colors"
98	            >
99	              − less
100	            </button>
101	          )}
102	
103	          {active.length > 0 && (
104	            <>
105	              <div className="bg-muted ml-2 flex rounded p-0.5">
106	                {(["any", "all"] as const).map((m) => (
107	                  <button
108	                    key={m}
109	                    type="button"
110	                    onClick={() =>
111	                      set("tagMatch", m === "any" ? null : m)
112	                    }
113	                    className={cn(
114	                      "rounded px-2 py-0.5 text-[10px] font-bold tracking-wider uppercase transition",
115	                      tagMatch === m
116	                        ? "bg-card text-foreground shadow-sm"
117	                        : "text-muted-foreground",
118	                    )}
119	                  >
120	                    {m}
121	                  </button>
122	                ))}
123	              </div>
124	              <button
125	                type="button"
126	                onClick={() => setMulti("tags", [])}
127	                className="border-border text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground inline-flex items-center gap-1 rounded-full border px-2.5 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold transition"
128	              >
129	                <X className="size-3" />
130	                Clear tags
131	              </button>
132	            </>
133	          )}
134	        </div>
135	      )}
136	    </div>
137	  );
138	}
139	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/validators/filters.ts",
  "old_string": "export function pageOffset(page: number | undefined) {\n  return ((page ?? 1) - 1) * PAGE_SIZE;\n}",
  "new_string": "export function pageOffset(page: number | undefined) {\n  return ((page ?? 1) - 1) * PAGE_SIZE;\n}\n\n/**\n * Inverse of `parseFilters`. Encodes typed filters back into URL search\n * params using the same conventions parseFilters consumes — comma-joined\n * arrays for `tags`, omitted keys for undefined / empty values.\n */\nexport function serializeFilters(filters: Partial<Filters>): URLSearchParams {\n  const params = new URLSearchParams();\n  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(filters)) {\n    if (value === undefined || value === \"\" || value === null) continue;\n    if (Array.isArray(value)) {\n      if (value.length > 0) params.set(key, value.join(\",\"));\n    } else {\n      params.set(key, String(value));\n    }\n  }\n  return params;\n}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/hooks/use-filters.ts",
  "content": "\"use client\";\n\nimport { useSearchParams } from \"next/navigation\";\nimport { useMemo, useTransition } from \"react\";\n\nimport { usePathname, useRouter } from \"@/lib/i18n/navigation\";\nimport {\n  parseFilters,\n  serializeFilters,\n  type Filters,\n} from \"@/lib/validators/filters\";\n\nexport type FilterUpdate = Partial<Filters>;\n\nexport interface UseFiltersResult {\n  filters: Filters;\n  update: (partial: FilterUpdate) => void;\n  pending: boolean;\n}\n\n// Build a Record<string, string | string[]> from URLSearchParams that\n// `parseFilters` knows how to consume (it splits comma-joined arrays itself).\nfunction searchParamsToInput(\n  searchParams: URLSearchParams,\n): Record<string, string | string[]> {\n  const out: Record<string, string | string[]> = {};\n  for (const [key, value] of searchParams.entries()) {\n    out[key] = value;\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\n/**\n * Typed filter state synced to the URL. Components read `filters` (parsed\n * via the validator, so invalid URL state silently becomes empty) and\n * write multi-key updates via `update(partial)`.\n *\n * - Setting a key to a value writes it to the URL.\n * - Setting a key to `undefined` removes it from the URL.\n * - Any update that does not include `page` resets pagination to its\n *   default (page 1) — filter changes shouldn't preserve a stale page.\n */\nexport function useFilters(): UseFiltersResult {\n  const router = useRouter();\n  const pathname = usePathname();\n  const searchParams = useSearchParams();\n  const [pending, startTransition] = useTransition();\n\n  const filters = useMemo(\n    () => parseFilters(searchParamsToInput(searchParams)),\n    [searchParams],\n  );\n\n  function update(partial: FilterUpdate) {\n    const next: FilterUpdate = { ...filters, ...partial };\n    if (!(\"page\" in partial)) {\n      next.page = undefined;\n    }\n    const params = serializeFilters(next);\n    const qs = params.toString();\n    startTransition(() => {\n      router.replace(qs ? `${pathname}?${qs}` : pathname, {\n        scroll: false,\n      } as Parameters<typeof router.replace>[1]);\n    });\n  }\n\n  return { filters, update, pending };\n}\n"
}

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nrefactor(filters): typed filter hook with single update method\n\nThe Filters concept lived in three places: validators/filters.ts owned\nthe type, search/query.ts projected it to SQL, but the URL projection\nwas implicit and untyped — useFilterUrl exposed get/getMulti/set/setMulti\nwith literal string keys, and each of the five filter components passed\n\"category\", \"scope\", \"tags\", etc as raw strings. A typo silently\nno-op'd because parseFilters drops unknown keys.\n\nNow:\n- lib/validators/filters.ts adds serializeFilters as the inverse of\n  parseFilters. Both URL projections live next to the type; the\n  comma-encoding for `tags` is no longer duplicated.\n- lib/hooks/use-filters.ts replaces use-filter-url.ts. Returns\n  { filters, update, pending } where filters is a parsed `Filters`\n  and update accepts `Partial<Filters>` — multi-key, typed, with\n  undefined meaning \"remove this key from the URL\".\n- The page-reset behavior is preserved: any update that doesn't\n  include `page` clears it from the URL.\n- The five filter components each migrate from get/set string keys\n  to filters.x / update({ x: ... }).\n\nTests: 6 cases for serializeFilters + 6 round-trip cases asserting\nparseFilters(serializeFilters(f)) === f for representative inputs\n(including the all-fields case).\nEOF\n)\" && git push -u origin refactor/typed-filters 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Commit and push"
}

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"refactor(filters): typed filter hook with single update method\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nThe Filters concept was real but its three projections lived in three places with inconsistent typing:\n\n- **`lib/validators/filters.ts`** — `Filters` type + `parseFilters` (URL → Filters). Source of truth.\n- **`lib/search/query.ts`** — `buildExtensionWhere` (Filters → SQL).\n- **`lib/hooks/use-filter-url.ts`** — generic, untyped `get/getMulti/set/setMulti`. The URL projection is implicit and lives across five filter components, each passing literal string keys (`\"category\"`, `\"scope\"`, `\"tags\"`, ...).\n\nA typo like `set(\"catgeory\", x)` silently no-op'd because `parseFilters` drops unknown keys. Comma-encoding for `tags` lived in two places (`parseFilters` and `getMulti`).\n\n## Design\n\nSingle home for both URL projections (parse + serialize) next to the type. Single typed hook for the components.\n\n```ts\n// lib/validators/filters.ts\nexport function parseFilters(input): Filters\nexport function serializeFilters(partial: Partial<Filters>): URLSearchParams  // new\n\n// lib/hooks/use-filters.ts (replaces use-filter-url.ts)\nexport function useFilters(): {\n  filters: Filters;                                // parsed, validated\n  update: (partial: Partial<Filters>) => void;     // multi-key, typed\n  pending: boolean;\n}\n```\n\n`update({ category: \"skills\" })` sets; `update({ category: undefined })` clears. Filter changes that omit `page` reset pagination — same behavior as the old `commit()`.\n\nThe five filter components migrate from `get(\"foo\")` / `set(\"foo\", null)` to `filters.foo` / `update({ foo: undefined })`.\n\n## Behaviour preserved\n\n- Filter changes reset `page` to 1 (rule: if `partial.page === undefined`, drop `page`).\n- Invalid URL state silently becomes empty filters (`parseFilters` already does this).\n- `router.replace` inside `useTransition` with `scroll: false` — same.\n- Comma-joined tags in the URL — same wire format, but encoding lives in one place now.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run lint` — clean\n- [x] `bun run typecheck` — clean\n- [x] `bun run test` — 162 tests pass (150 → 162; 12 new on `serializeFilters` + parse↔serialize round-trip including the all-fields case)\n- [ ] CI runs on this PR\n- [ ] Smoke-check: clicking a category chip updates URL and grid, selecting a tag adds to comma-joined `?tags=` param, clearing all tags removes the param, page resets to 1 when any non-page filter changes\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Open PR"
}

=== lib/hooks/use-filters.ts:29 (id=3206123977) ===
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Preserve duplicate query keys instead of overwriting them.**

Line 28 currently drops earlier values for the same key. That breaks multi-value URLs (e.g., repeated `tags`) and bypasses `parseFilters` support for `string[]`.

 
<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
 function searchParamsToInput(
   searchParams: URLSearchParams,
 ): Record<string, string | string[]> {
   const out: Record<string, string | string[]> = {};
   for (const [key, value] of searchParams.entries()) {
-    out[key] = value;
+    const prev = out[key];
+    if (prev === undefined) {
+      out[key] = value;
+    } else if (Array.isArray(prev)) {
+      prev.push(value);
+    } else {
+      out[key] = [prev, value];
+    }
   }
   return out;
 }
```
</details>

> AGENT

One actionable comment, valid: `searchParamsToInput` overwrites duplicate keys. A URL with `?tags=a&tags=b` (two separate params instead of one comma-joined) would yield `{tags: "b"}` and silently drop `"a"`. Our own `serializeFilters` always emits comma-joined, but external links / bookmarks could send the array form, and `parseFilters` already supports it (validators/filters.ts:38-46).

Same pre-existing issue in the old `useFilterUrl.get()`, but the fix lives in the new code. Applying.

id: toolu_018f8i8r7jaEdncnubAwomMf
```
25	
26	The App Router tree is locale-segmented. URLs are always prefixed (`/en/...`, `/zh/...`); there is no implicit default.
27	
28	```text
29	app/
30	├── layout.tsx                 # html/body, font loaders, theme cookie
31	├── [locale]/
32	│   ├── layout.tsx             # next-intl provider, AppShell (sidebar + topbar)
33	│   ├── page.tsx               # home (featured + trending)
34	│   ├── extensions/
35	│   │   ├── page.tsx           # browse (filter bar + grid)
36	│   │   └── [slug]/page.tsx    # detail (README + metadata panel)
37	│   ├── publish/
38	│   │   ├── page.tsx           # dashboard (my extensions)
39	│   │   └── new/page.tsx       # upload wizard
40	│   ├── (auth)/sign-in,sign-up
41	│   └── cli/auth/page.tsx      # device-code authorization UI
42	└── api/                       # see "API surfaces" below
43	```
44	
45	### RSC vs client islands
46	
47	Server components render the static shell, fetch data via Drizzle, and stream HTML. Client components are reserved for interactive bits — filter chips, the install button, the upload wizard, the user menu — and are marked with `"use client"`. The pattern is "server frame, client islands."
48	
49	The filter bar (`components/filters/filter-bar.tsx`) is a server component but composes client islands (`FilterChips`, `SortSelect`, `DeptPicker`, `TagDrawer`). Each island reads/writes URL search params via `useFilterUrl`, so filter state lives in the URL — no global store, no context, and back/forward works.
50	
51	### i18n
52	
53	Locales live under `lib/i18n/messages/{en,zh}.json`. `next-intl` is configured with `localePrefix: "always"`. Static UI strings come from `useTranslations(...)`; bilingual content (extension `name` / `nameZh`, tag labels) is column-per-language in Postgres and resolved at the component level via `tagLabel(...)` / direct field access.
54	
55	### Theme
56	
57	`Editorial Ivory` (default) and `Dark`. The active theme is stored in a cookie (`theme`), read in the root layout, and applied as a `data-theme` attribute. The toggle (`ThemeSwitch`) writes the cookie and triggers `router.refresh()`.
58	
59	## Request flows
60	
61	### Browse
62	
63	```text
64	GET /en/extensions?category=skills&dept=eng.cloud&sort=stars
65	  └─ app/[locale]/extensions/page.tsx (RSC)
66	      ├─ parseFilters(searchParams)
67	      ├─ Promise.all([listExtensions, countFilteredExtensions, getTagsWithCounts])
68	      └─ <FilterBar tags={tags} /> + <ExtGrid items={items} />
69	```
70	
71	`buildExtensionWhere` / `buildExtensionOrder` in `lib/search/query.ts` compose Drizzle SQL clauses from filters; they're pure (no DB call), unit-tested, and shared with `/api/v1/extensions` so web and CLI see the same listing semantics.
72	
73	### Detail
74	
```

/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/docs/architecture.md:117:`lib/extensions/state.ts` owns every transition of `extensionVersions.status`, `files.scanStatus`, and `extensions.visibility`. Three functions:
/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/docs/architecture.md:138:Both surfaces are thin wrappers around `recordInstall` in `lib/installs/record.ts`, which owns: extension lookup (by `id` from the web button or `slug` from the CLI), version resolution (an omitted version means "latest published" — `extension_versions` ordered by `publishedAt DESC` among `status='ready'`), the `installs` row insert, the `installed` collection upsert, and the atomic `downloadsCount` bump — all three writes go through `db.transaction`. Each call records a row, so `downloadsCount` is total install events; the `installed` collection is a separate, idempotent membership concept used by the UI's "Installed" view. Failures throw a typed `InstallError` (`extension_not_found`, `no_published_version`); wrappers translate to their surface (action union or HTTP status).

## Audit summary

**Docs — one gap found, now fixed:**

- `plan.md` (585 lines) — phase markers; could note the four refactors landed but I'd treat that as your editorial call, not a bug.

**Tests — no gaps from the refactors:**
- Each refactor shipped with tests for its new surface: `lib/installs/record.test.ts`, `lib/extensions/state.test.ts`, `lib/validators/manifest.test.ts`, `lib/validators/filters.test.ts`. 167 tests total, was ~120 before.
- **Pre-existing gaps** that the refactors didn't fill (and weren't created by them): Inngest job functions, HTTP route handlers, integration test for `useFilters` hook. The first two are intentional per project convention (e2e covers them). The hook integration test would need happy-dom + Next.js mocking; the pure helpers underneath are well-tested.

558	
559	## 11. Locked decisions (the 10 that drove the plan)
560	
561	These were decided in the planning conversation. Update this section in the same commit when any decision changes.
562	
563	1. **Multi-tenant schema, single-tenant UI** — `organizations` table populated, single org for v1, no org switcher in UI. Multi-org flip is middleware-only later.
564	2. **Auth library**: Better Auth.
565	3. **Background jobs**: Inngest.
566	4. **Themes**: Editorial Ivory + Dark only. Mono Clean dropped.
567	5. **Bundle storage**: Cloudflare R2.
568	6. **Install semantics**: real machine-side install via the Agent CLI. Web records install events; CLI does the work. CLI bumped to **P0**.
569	7. **Sign-up policy**: open.
570	8. **Locale URLs**: always-prefixed (`/en/...`, `/zh/...`).
571	9. **Detail page README**: raw markdown stored, rendered server-side with `react-markdown` + `rehype-sanitize`. Manifest metadata rendered as side panel.
572	10. **CLI strategy**: agent-agnostic with Claude-first defaults. Extensions declare per-agent install paths in the manifest; users override via `agentcenter config`.
573	
574	---
575	
576	## 12. Prototype-to-Next mapping (load-bearing)
577	
578	- `T` object in prototype HTML → split into `messages/en.json` + `messages/zh.json`, preserving keys verbatim.
579	- `DEPARTMENTS` constant → seeded into `departments` table; the dotted-path id is **kept verbatim as the primary key**. This is the load-bearing schema decision — it makes descendant filtering a single `LIKE` predicate.
580	- `FUNC_TAXONOMY` → static TS in `lib/taxonomy.ts`, not a DB table. Product-defined, rarely changes.
581	- `TAG_LABELS` → seeded into `tags` table.
582	- `EXTENSIONS` array → `scripts/seed.ts` rows; `dept`/`scope`/`funcCat`/`subCat` map to typed columns.
583	- `MY_DEPT_ID` constant → comes from authenticated user's `defaultDeptId`.
584	- `isDescendant(deptId, ancestorId)` → moves into the SQL query (`OR LIKE 'ancestor.%'`), no client-side recursion.
585	- `FilterAreaB` (Mode B drawer) is the only filter mode to build; A and C were prototype scaffolding.
```

Read plan.md. v1 (Phases 0–13) appears done — all the refactors we just did were inside Phase 7–12. The plan's P1 (post-v1) list:

| Item | What it actually means in this codebase |
|---|---|
| **CLI installers for MCP / slash / plugins** | Today only `installSkill` exists. The dispatcher in `cli/src/installers/index.ts` is the hypothetical-seam I flagged in the original survey — making it real means writing 3 adapters that each consume the `[install.<agent>]` section already documented in `manifest-spec.md`. |
| **Ratings & reviews UI** | Schema (`ratings` table) is already there; needs detail-page UI + a server action. |
| **Publisher dashboard** | Stub at `/publish/page.tsx`. Needs install metrics, version diffs, version table. |
| **Trending calculation** | Needs `view_events` tracking + a recalc job. Currently `filter=trending` uses a hardcoded `downloads > 50000` threshold. |
| **Collection sharing** | New surface; not started. |
| **Admin moderation** | New surface; not started. |
| **Homebrew CLI distribution** | Distribution-only. |

My read: **CLI installers is the right next slice**. It turns the hypothetical seam into a real one (the *exact* "two adapters = real seam" rule from the architecture skill), each adapter is small and self-contained, the manifest schema already supports it (PR #6 made the schemas spec-aligned), and you ship a real new product capability — users on MCP/slash/plugins extensions can actually install them.

Ratings/reviews is the runner-up — small, visible, schema's ready. Would ship a feature users see directly.

Trending, dashboard, collection sharing are bigger. Skip for now.

Pick one and I'll grill the design (which categories first, where to draw the per-agent path resolution, what to do about `agentcenter config set` overrides). What's your pick?

```
1	"use client";
2	
3	import { useSearchParams } from "next/navigation";
4	import { useMemo, useTransition } from "react";
5	
6	import { usePathname, useRouter } from "@/lib/i18n/navigation";
7	import {
8	  parseFilters,
9	  searchParamsToInput,
10	  serializeFilters,
11	  type Filters,
12	} from "@/lib/validators/filters";
13	
14	export type FilterUpdate = Partial<Filters>;
15	
16	export interface UseFiltersResult {
17	  filters: Filters;
18	  update: (partial: FilterUpdate) => void;
19	  pending: boolean;
20	}
21	
22	/**
23	 * Typed filter state synced to the URL. Components read `filters` (parsed
24	 * via the validator, so invalid URL state silently becomes empty) and
25	 * write multi-key updates via `update(partial)`.
26	 *
27	 * - Setting a key to a value writes it to the URL.
28	 * - Setting a key to `undefined` removes it from the URL.
29	 * - Any update that does not include `page` resets pagination to its
30	 *   default (page 1) — filter changes shouldn't preserve a stale page.
31	 */
32	export function useFilters(): UseFiltersResult {
33	  const router = useRouter();
34	  const pathname = usePathname();
35	  const searchParams = useSearchParams();
36	  const [pending, startTransition] = useTransition();
37	
38	  const filters = useMemo(
39	    () => parseFilters(searchParamsToInput(searchParams)),
40	    [searchParams],
41	  );
42	
43	  function update(partial: FilterUpdate) {
44	    const next: FilterUpdate = { ...filters, ...partial };
45	    if (!("page" in partial)) {
46	      next.page = undefined;
47	    }
48	    const params = serializeFilters(next);
49	    const qs = params.toString();
50	    startTransition(() => {
51	      router.replace(qs ? `${pathname}?${qs}` : pathname, {
52	        scroll: false,
53	      } as Parameters<typeof router.replace>[1]);
54	    });
55	  }
56	
57	  return { filters, update, pending };
58	}
59	
```

vi.fn();\nlet currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams();\n\nvi.mock(\"next/navigation\", () => ({\n  useSearchParams: () => currentSearchParams,\n}));\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/i18n/navigation\", () => ({\n  useRouter: () => ({ replace: replaceMock, push: vi.fn() }),\n  usePathname: () => \"/en/extensions\",\n}));\n\nimport { useFilters } from \"@/lib/hooks/use-filters\";\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  replaceMock.mockClear();\n  currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams();\n});\n\n// Helper: extract the search-params portion of the URL the hook wrote.\nfunction lastReplacedQuery(): URLSearchParams {\n  const url = replaceMock.mock.calls.at(-1)?.[0] as string | undefined;\n  if (!url) return new URLSearchParams();\n  const qs = url.includes(\"?\") ? url.split(\"?\")[1] : \"\";\n  return new URLSearchParams(qs);\n}\n\ndescribe(\"useFilters: read\", () => {\n  it(\"returns empty filters when the URL has no params\", () => {\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useFilters());\n cleared (no '?' in URL)\", () => {\n    currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams(\"category=skills\");\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useFilters());\n    act(() => result.current.update({ category: undefined }));\n\n    const url = replaceMock.mock.calls.at(-1)?.[0];\n    expect(url).toBe(\"/en/extensions\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"useFilters: page-reset behavior\", () => {\n  it(\"clears page from the URL when a non-page filter changes\", () => {\n    currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams(\"category=skills&page=4\");\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useFilters());\n    act(() => result.current.update({ scope: \"personal\" }));\n\n    expect(lastReplacedQuery().has(\"page\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it(\"preserves page when the update explicitly includes page\", () => {\n    currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams(\"category=skills\");\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useFilters());\n    act(() => result.current.update({ page: 3 }));\n\n    expect(lastReplacedQuery().get(\"page\")).toBe(\"3\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"clears page even when the update is a no-op-looking change\", () => {\n    // User clicks the chip they're already on — still counts as \"filter\n    // changed\" from the URL's perspective and should reset page.\n    currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams(\"category=skills&page=2\");\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useFilters());\n    act(() => result.current.update({ category: \"skills\" }));\n\n    expect(lastReplacedQuery().has(\"page\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n});\n"
}

<details>
<summary>🧹 Nitpick comments (1)</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>lib/hooks/use-filters.test.ts (1)</summary><blockquote>

`5-15`: _⚡ Quick win_

**Use `vi.hoisted()` for mock-shared variables to follow Vitest's documented contract.**

`vi.mock` is hoisted to the top of the file — meaning it runs before all other module-level code — and the docs explicitly state "you cannot use any variables inside the factory that are defined outside the factory."

`replaceMock` and `currentSearchParams` are declared at module scope and referenced inside the `vi.mock` factory closures. The code currently works in practice because both variables are only accessed lazily (inside the nested `() => currentSearchParams` / `() => ({ replace: replaceMock })` arrow functions, which run at test time after the module initialises). However, this relies on an implementation detail of Vitest's transform and is fragile against future changes to the hoisting mechanism.

The prescribed fix is to declare such variables via `vi.hoisted()`, which ensures they are initialised before any mock factory runs:

<details>
<summary>♻️ Proposed refactor using <code>vi.hoisted()</code></summary>

```diff
-const replaceMock = vi.fn();
-let currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams();
+const { replaceMock, getSearchParams, setSearchParams } = vi.hoisted(() => {
+  let _currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams();
+  return {
+    replaceMock: vi.fn(),
+    getSearchParams: () => _currentSearchParams,
+    setSearchParams: (p: URLSearchParams) => { _currentSearchParams = p; },
+  };
+});

 vi.mock("next/navigation", () => ({
-  useSearchParams: () => currentSearchParams,
+  useSearchParams: () => getSearchParams(),
 }));

 vi.mock("@/lib/i18n/navigation", () => ({
   useRouter: () => ({ replace: replaceMock, push: vi.fn() }),
   usePathname: () => "/en/extensions",
 }));
```

Then update `beforeEach` and the tests that assign to `currentSearchParams`:

```diff
 beforeEach(() => {
   replaceMock.mockClear();
-  currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams();
+  setSearchParams(new URLSearchParams());
 });
```

```diff
-  currentSearchParams = new URLSearchParams("category=skills&scope=personal");
+  setSearchParams(new URLSearchParams("category=skills&scope=personal"));
```

(Apply the same `setSearchParams(...)` swap to all the other test-level `currentSearchParams = ...` assignments.)
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/CHANGELOG.md",
  "content": "# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to AgentCenter and the bundled `@agentcenter/cli` are\nrecorded here. Versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org).\n\n## [0.2.0] — 2026-05-08\n\nInternal-quality release. No new product features; four targeted refactors\nthat consolidated duplicated logic, tightened correctness, and removed\ndead code, plus the matching test additions and doc refreshes.\n\n### Refactored\n\n- **Install recording** (#4) — Web button (`installExtension` server\n  action) and CLI route (`POST /api/v1/installs`) now both delegate to\n  one `recordInstall` module in `lib/installs/record.ts`. The module\n  owns extension lookup (by `id` or `slug`), version resolution\n  (omitted ⇒ latest published, ordered by `publishedAt DESC,\n  createdAt DESC`), the `installs` insert, the `Installed` collection\n  upsert, and the atomic `downloadsCount` increment — all three writes\n  inside one `db.transaction`. Throws typed `InstallError`; wrappers\n  translate to surface (action union or HTTP status).\n- **Version lifecycle** (#4) — Three transitions of `extensionVersions.status`,\n  `files.scanStatus`, and `extensions.visibility` consolidated in\n  `lib/extensions/state.ts`: `submit(versionId)` (pending→scanning),\n  `recordScanResult(versionId, fileId, result)` (scanning→ready|rejected),\n  and `publishVersion(versionId)` (ready→published, stamps `publishedAt`).\n  All three enforce source-state preconditions, throwing\n  `VersionStateError` when refused; `scan-bundle.ts` catches and treats\n  as no-op so duplicate Inngest deliveries don't poison terminal state.\n- **Manifest schema** (#6) — `lib/validators/manifest.ts` exports a\n  single `ExtensionManifestCore` mirroring `docs/manifest-spec.md`. The\n  form schema (`ManifestFormSchema`) and bundle schema\n  (`BundleManifestSchema`) both derive from it. Closes drift between\n  the two old schemas (slug max 80 vs 64, description 300 vs 280, name\n  min 2 vs 1, missing fields).\n- **Filter hook** (#8) — `useFilterUrl`'s untyped `get/set/getMulti/setMulti`\n  replaced by typed `useFilters()` returning `{ filters, update, pending }`.\n  String-key typos at the component layer are now compile errors.\n  Comma-encoding for `tags` lives in one place.\n\n### Behaviour changes\n\n- **`installs.version` no longer contains the literal string `\"latest\"`.**\n  Web installs now record the resolved semver. The CLI route still\n  accepts `\"latest\"` from older clients and translates it to \"resolve\n  internally.\"\n- **`downloadsCount` semantics shift from \"distinct users who installed\"\n  to \"total install events.\"** Every install records a row and bumps\n  the counter; the `Installed` collection is now a separate idempotent\n  membership concept used by the UI's \"Installed\" view.\n- **CLI `downloadsCount` increment is now atomic** — the route used to\n  do read-then-write, which could lose updates under concurrent\n  installs. Both surfaces now go through `sql\\`+ 1\\``.\n- **Bundle scan validation tightens to the spec.** Bundles with 1-char\n  names, missing descriptions, or slugs that fail the regex are now\n  rejected at scan time; previously the form schema enforced these,\n  the bundle schema didn't.\n- **Form description is now required** to match the manifest spec.\n\n### Fixed\n\n- **Dead `to_tsvector` UPDATE removed from `reindex-search.ts`** — the\n  `extensions.search_vector` column has been a Postgres `GENERATED\n  ALWAYS … STORED` column since the P12 migration, so the hand-rolled\n  UPDATE always threw and the catch-block fallback (plain visibility\n  flip) was the only path that ran. Postgres maintains the column from\n  source content automatically; no application write is needed.\n- **`useFilters` preserves duplicate query keys** — a URL with\n  `?tags=a&tags=b` (two separate params instead of one comma-joined)\n  used to silently drop the first value. The same pre-existing issue\n  was in the old `useFilterUrl.get()`.\n- **Form `validate()` mirrors schema constraints** — short slugs (1–2\n  chars) and 1-char names used to pass the form's hand-rolled\n  `validate()`, then fail server-side. Now caught inline.\n\n### CLI\n\n- The CLI's response handling for `POST /api/v1/installs` continues to\n  use a fire-and-forget pattern; the response shape change\n  (`{ ok, alreadyInstalled }` → `{ ok, installId, isFirstInstall, version }`)\n  is documented in `docs/api.md` but does not affect the bundled CLI.\n\n### Tests\n\n182 tests (was ~120 at start of session). New coverage on:\n`lib/installs/record.test.ts`, `lib/extensions/state.test.ts`,\n`lib/validators/manifest.test.ts` (rewritten),\n`lib/validators/filters.test.ts` (expanded), `lib/hooks/use-filters.test.ts`.\n\n## [0.1.0] — 2026-04 (initial)\n\nInternal v1 — Phases 0–13 of `docs/plan.md`. Browse, detail, publish,\nauth, collections, install events, public registry API, CLI v1\n(skills only), upload wizard, background jobs, FTS.\n"
}
